(25)
where in the penultimate inequality, we used the fact that 11−x−1≤2​x\frac{1}{1-x}-1\leq 2x for x∈[0,1/2]x\in[0,1/2] and that ϵ4≤b/4\epsilon_{4}\leq b/4, and the final inequality uses the definitions of ϵ3\epsilon_{3} and ϵ4\epsilon_{4}. If we set the first attention layer 𝖠𝗍𝗍𝗇θattn(1)\mathsf{Attn}_{\theta^{(1)}_{\mathrm{attn}}} to be the identity map (i.e. set the parameters of the attention layer to zero), and let Zˇ(n)≔𝖥𝖥𝖭θffn(2)∘𝖠𝗍𝗍𝗇θattn(2)∘𝖥𝖥𝖭θffn(1)∘𝖠𝗍𝗍𝗇θattn(1)​(Zin(n,dmodel))\check{Z}^{(n)}\coloneqq\mathsf{FFN}_{\theta_{\mathrm{ffn}}^{(2)}}\circ\mathsf{Attn}_{\theta^{(2)}_{\mathrm{attn}}}\circ\mathsf{FFN}_{\theta_{\mathrm{ffn}}^{(1)}}\circ\mathsf{Attn}_{\theta^{(1)}_{\mathrm{attn}}}(Z_{\mathrm{in}}^{(n,{d_{\mathrm{model}}})}), then by (25) and the triangle inequality, we have on ℰ0∩ℰ1∩ℰ2∩ℰ3\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3} that for all n∈[N]n\in[N]
|Zˇn+1,d+1(n)−gπ​(X,𝒟n)|=|[𝖥𝖥𝖭θffn(2)​(Z¯(n))]n+1,d+1−gπ​(X,𝒟n)|≤ϵ2.\displaystyle\Bigl|\check{Z}^{(n)}_{n+1,d+1}-g_{\pi}(X,\mathcal{D}_{n})\Bigr|=\Bigl|\bigl[\mathsf{FFN}_{\theta_{\mathrm{ffn}}^{(2)}}(\bar{Z}^{(n)})\bigr]_{n+1,d+1}-g_{\pi}(X,\mathcal{D}_{n})\Bigr|\leq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{2}.
(26)
For n∈[N]n\in[N], let Pˇ(n)\check{P}^{(n)} be the distribution of the last row of Zˇ(n)\check{Z}^{(n)}. By applying Lemma 11 with μ=1N​∑n=1NPˇ(n)\mu=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\check{P}^{(n)} therein, there exists an FFN layer 𝖥𝖥𝖭θffn(3)\mathsf{FFN}_{\theta_{\mathrm{ffn}}^{(3)}} such that the event
ℰ4≔{maxn∈[N]|[𝖥𝖥𝖭θffn(3)(Zˇ(n))]n+1,d+1−𝖼𝗅𝗂𝗉R(Zˇn+1,d+1(n))|≤ϵ/4}\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}\coloneqq\Bigl\{\max_{n\in[N]}\Bigl|\bigl[\mathsf{FFN}_{\theta_{\mathrm{ffn}}^{(3)}}(\check{Z}^{(n)})\bigr]_{n+1,d+1}-\mathsf{clip}_{R}(\check{Z}^{(n)}_{n+1,d+1})\Bigr|\leq\sqrt{\epsilon}/4\Bigr\}
has probability at least 1−δ1-\delta. Finally, let 𝖠𝗍𝗍𝗇θattn(3)\mathsf{Attn}_{\theta^{(3)}_{\mathrm{attn}}} be the identity map and let
𝖳𝖥≔𝖥𝖥𝖭θffn(3)∘𝖠𝗍𝗍𝗇θattn(3)∘𝖥𝖥𝖭θffn(2)∘𝖠𝗍𝗍𝗇θattn(2)∘𝖥𝖥𝖭θffn(1)∘𝖠𝗍𝗍𝗇θattn(1).\mathsf{TF}\coloneqq\mathsf{FFN}_{\theta_{\mathrm{ffn}}^{(3)}}\circ\mathsf{Attn}_{\theta^{(3)}_{\mathrm{attn}}}\circ\mathsf{FFN}_{\theta_{\mathrm{ffn}}^{(2)}}\circ\mathsf{Attn}_{\theta^{(2)}_{\mathrm{attn}}}\circ\mathsf{FFN}_{\theta_{\mathrm{ffn}}^{(1)}}\circ\mathsf{Attn}_{\theta^{(1)}_{\mathrm{attn}}}.
Since ‖gπ‖∞≤R\|g_{\pi}\|_{\infty}\leq R, we have by (26) that on ℰ≔ℰ0∩ℰ1∩ℰ2∩ℰ3∩ℰ4\mathcal{E}\coloneqq\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4}, for all n∈[N]n\in[N],
|𝖱𝖾𝖺𝖽∘𝖳𝖥⁡(Zin(n,dmodel))−gπ​(X,𝒟n)|\displaystyle\bigl|\mathsf{Read}\circ\mathsf{TF}(Z_{\mathrm{in}}^{(n,{d_{\mathrm{model}}})})-g_{\pi}(X,\mathcal{D}_{n})\bigr|
≤|[𝖥𝖥𝖭θffn(3)​(Zˇ(n))]n+1,d+1−𝖼𝗅𝗂𝗉R​(Zˇn+1,d+1(n))|+|𝖼𝗅𝗂𝗉R​(Zˇn+1,d+1(n))−gπ​(X,𝒟n)|≤3​ϵ4.\displaystyle\leq\Bigl|\bigl[\mathsf{FFN}_{\theta_{\mathrm{ffn}}^{(3)}}(\check{Z}^{(n)})\bigr]_{n+1,d+1}-\mathsf{clip}_{R}(\check{Z}^{(n)}_{n+1,d+1})\Bigr|+\Bigl|\mathsf{clip}_{R}(\check{Z}^{(n)}_{n+1,d+1})-g_{\pi}(X,\mathcal{D}_{n})\Bigr|\leq\frac{3\sqrt{\epsilon}}{4}.
By a union bound, the event ℰ\mathcal{E} has probability at least 1−5​δ1-5\delta. We conclude that
maxn∈[N]⁡𝔼P∼π,(𝒟n,X,Y)|P∼P⊗(n+1)​{(𝖱𝖾𝖺𝖽∘𝖳𝖥⁡(X,𝒟n)−gπ​(X,𝒟n))2}≤9​ϵ16+5​R2​δ≤ϵ,\displaystyle\max_{n\in[N]}\mathbb{E}_{P\sim\pi,(\mathcal{D}_{n},X,Y)|P\sim P^{\otimes(n+1)}}\Bigl\{\bigl(\mathsf{Read}\circ\mathsf{TF}(X,\mathcal{D}_{n})-g_{\pi}(X,\mathcal{D}_{n})\bigr)^{2}\Bigr\}\leq\frac{9\epsilon}{16}+5R^{2}\delta\leq\epsilon,
as required.
∎
C.2 Proof of Proposition 3
The main idea behind the proof of Proposition 3 is to use empirical process theory to upper bound ℰ⁡(f~T)\mathcal{E}(\widetilde{f}_{T}) by the approximation error and a complexity measure of ℱ\mathcal{F}. In the literature, the complexity measure is often taken to be the covering number with respect to the uniform norm, and upper bounds on this covering number for Transformers are available, provided the input matrix and parameters are bounded. There, the proof strategy is to argue that for any input matrix from a bounded set, the output of the Transformer with bounded parameters is uniformly Lipschitz in its parameters. However, this argument does not work in our setting, since our input matrix and parameters are unbounded. Instead, we use the pseudo-dimension of ℱ\mathcal{F} (see the definition below) as a complexity measure and show that the pseudo-dimension of Transformers is finite. This result (Lemma 15 below) may be of independent interest.
Definition 5 (VC-dimension and pseudo-dimension).
Let XX be a set and let 𝒞\mathcal{C} be a collection of subsets of XX. For S≔{x1,…,xn}⊆XS\coloneqq\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\}\subseteq X, we say that 𝒞\mathcal{C} shatters SS if for every T⊆ST\subseteq S, there exists C∈𝒞C\in\mathcal{C} such that C∩S=TC\cap S=T. The VC-dimension of 𝒞\mathcal{C} is the cardinality of the largest subset of XX that can be shattered by 𝒞\mathcal{C}.
Now let 𝒢\mathcal{G} be a set of functions from 𝒳\mathcal{X} to ℝ\mathbb{R}. The pseudo-dimension Pdim⁡(𝒢)\mathrm{Pdim}(\mathcal{G}) of 𝒢\mathcal{G} is the VC-dimension of its subgraph class {{(x,y)∈𝒳×ℝ:y≤g⁡(x)}:g∈𝒢}\bigl\{\{(x,y)\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathbb{R}:y\leq g(x)\}:g\in\mathcal{G}\bigr\}.
Lemma 13 below controls ℰ⁡(f~T)\mathcal{E}(\widetilde{f}_{T}) in terms of the approximation error and pseudo-dimension of ℱ\mathcal{F}.
Lemma 13.
Under the setting of Proposition 3, there exists C>0C>0, depending only on ‖ξ‖ψ2\|\xi\|_{\psi_{2}} and RR, such that for T≥2T\geq 2,
ℰ⁡(f~T)≤2​inff∈ℱ𝔼P∼π,(𝒟n,X,Y)|P∼P⊗(n+1)​{(f⁡(X,𝒟n)−gπ​(X,𝒟n))2}+C​log3⁡(T)⋅Pdim⁡(ℱ)T.\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\widetilde{f}_{T})\leq 2\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}_{P\sim\pi,\,(\mathcal{D}_{n},X,Y)|P\sim P^{\otimes(n+1)}}\Bigl\{\bigl(f(X,\mathcal{D}_{n})-g_{\pi}(X,\mathcal{D}_{n})\bigr)^{2}\Bigr\}+\frac{C\log^{3}(T)\cdot\mathrm{Pdim}(\mathcal{F})}{T}.
Proof.
Let P∼πP\sim\pi, (𝒟n,X,Y)|P∼P⊗(n+1)(\mathcal{D}_{n},X,Y)|P\sim P^{\otimes(n+1)} and let (𝒟n(t),X(t),Y(t))t∈[T](\mathcal{D}_{n}^{(t)},X^{(t)},Y^{(t)})_{t\in[T]} be independent copies of (𝒟n,X,Y)(\mathcal{D}_{n},X,Y).
For t∈[T]t\in[T], let Z(t)≔(X(t),𝒟n(t))Z^{(t)}\coloneqq(X^{(t)},\mathcal{D}_{n}^{(t)}) and let Z≔(X,𝒟n)Z\coloneqq(X,\mathcal{D}_{n}).
Now note that gπ​(Z)=𝔼​(Y|Z)g_{\pi}(Z)=\mathbb{E}(Y\,|\,Z),
f^T∈argminf∈ℱ1T​∑t=1T(Y(t)−f⁡(Z(t)))2\displaystyle\widehat{f}_{T}\in\argmin_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bigl(Y^{(t)}-f(Z^{(t)})\bigr)^{2}
and
ℰ⁡(f~T)=𝔼⁡{(Y−𝖼𝗅𝗂𝗉R​f^T​(Z))2}−𝔼⁡{(Y−gπ​(Z))2}.\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\widetilde{f}_{T})=\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\bigl(Y-\mathsf{clip}_{R}\widehat{f}_{T}(Z)\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}-\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\bigl(Y-g_{\pi}(Z)\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}.
We have therefore reduced our problem to bounding the excess risk of a nonparametric regression estimator in a problem with bounded regression function and sub-Gaussian noise. The conclusion now follows by applying, for example, Ma, Wang and Samworth (2025, Theorem 1) with the following minor modifications: (i) we ignore their Ω\Omega vectors since our data do not have missing values, (ii) by inspecting their proof, we may truncate f^T\widehat{f}_{T} at level RR since this is a known bound on the regression function, (iii) the optimisation error term (the first term in their upper bound) is zero since we consider the empirical risk minimiser, and (iv) by inspecting their proof and using Györfi et al. (2002, Theorem 9.4), we can replace the third term in their upper bound by C​log3⁡(T)⋅Pdim⁡(ℱ)T\frac{C\log^{3}(T)\cdot\mathrm{Pdim}(\mathcal{F})}{T}, where C>0C>0 depends only on ‖ξ‖ψ2\|\xi\|_{\psi_{2}} and RR.
∎
Next we will show that the class of Transformers with fixed architecture has finite pseudo-dimension. The proof uses terminology and results from model theory, a branch of mathematical logic. Since this field will probably be unfamiliar to most readers, we now provide a minimal glossary (sufficient for our proof) of the relevant terms; we refer the readers to Marker (2002) for a formal introduction to model theory. We will be concerned with an object called a structure, which is a set equipped with a collection of distinguished functions, relations and elements that together give meaning to the symbols of a formal language. Our structure of interest is ℝexp,Φ≔(ℝ;0,1,+,−,⋅,<,exp,Φ)\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}\coloneqq(\mathbb{R};0,1,+,-,\cdot,<,\exp,\Phi), where ⋅\cdot refers to multiplication, exp:ℝ→(0,∞)\exp:\mathbb{R}\to(0,\infty) refers to the exponential function and Φ:ℝ→[0,1]\Phi:\mathbb{R}\to[0,1] refers to the distribution function of the standard Gaussian distribution. Our goal is to show that Transformers can be defined within ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}, in a sense that we will make precise below.
Given a countable set 𝒱\mathcal{V} of variable symbols, which we intrepret as syntactic placeholders, the set of terms in the structure ℝexp,Φ≔(ℝ;0,1,+,−,⋅,<,exp,Φ)\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}\coloneqq(\mathbb{R};0,1,+,-,\cdot,<,\exp,\Phi) is the smallest set 𝒯\mathcal{T} such that (i) 0,1∈𝒯0,1\in\mathcal{T}, (ii) 𝒱⊆𝒯\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{T}, (iii) if t1,t2∈𝒯t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}, then t1+t2,t1−t2,t1⋅t2∈𝒯t_{1}+t_{2},\,t_{1}-t_{2},\,t_{1}\cdot t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}, and (iv) if t∈𝒯t\in\mathcal{T}, then exp⁡(t),Φ⁡(t)∈𝒯\exp(t),\Phi(t)\in\mathcal{T}. We say that φ\varphi is an atomic formula in ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi} if φ\varphi is either t1=t2t_{1}=t_{2} or t1<t2t_{1}<t_{2}, for some t1,t2∈𝒯t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathcal{T}. The set of (first-order) formulas 𝒲\mathcal{W} in (the language of) ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi} is the smallest set such that (i) every atomic formula is in 𝒲\mathcal{W}, (ii) if φ,ψ∈𝒲\varphi,\psi\in\mathcal{W}, then (¬φ),(φ∨ψ),(φ∧ψ)∈𝒲(\neg\varphi),(\varphi\vee\psi),(\varphi\wedge\psi)\in\mathcal{W}, where the logic symbolds ¬,∨,∧\neg,\vee,\wedge stand for negation, disjunction and conjunction, and (iii) if φ∈𝒲\varphi\in\mathcal{W} and v∈𝒱v\in\mathcal{V}, then ∃v​φ\exists v\;\varphi and ∀v​φ\forall v\;\varphi are in 𝒲\mathcal{W}. For a formula φ∈𝒲\varphi\in\mathcal{W}, we say that a variable vv is free in φ\varphi if it is not inside a ∃v\exists v or ∀v\forall v quantifier; e.g. v1v_{1} is free in the formula (v1=0)∨(v1>0)(v_{1}=0)\vee(v_{1}>0), but it is not free in the formula ∃v1​v2⋅v2=v1\exists v_{1}\;v_{2}\cdot v_{2}=v_{1}. When a formula φ\varphi has free variables v1,…,vnv_{1},\ldots,v_{n}, we will often write φ⁡(v1,…,vn)\varphi(v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}) to make the free variables explicit. Now, letting φ⁡(v1,…,vn)∈𝒲\varphi(v_{1},\ldots,v_{n})\in\mathcal{W} be a formula and a=(a1,…,an)∈ℝna=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}, we write ℝexp,Φ⊧φ⁡(a)\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}\models\varphi(a) (we read ‘ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi} entails φ⁡(a)\varphi(a)’ or ‘ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi} models φ⁡(a)\varphi(a)’) if φ⁡(a)\varphi(a) is true in ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}, and otherwise we write ℝexp,Φ⊧̸φ⁡(a)\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}\not\models\varphi(a). For example, if φ⁡(v1,v2)\varphi(v_{1},v_{2}) is the formula (v1=v2)∨(v1+v2=0)(v_{1}=v_{2})\vee(v_{1}+v_{2}=0), then ℝexp,Φ⊧φ⁡(1,1)\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}\models\varphi(1,1) and ℝexp,Φ⊧φ⁡(−1,1)\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}\models\varphi(-1,1), but ℝexp,Φ⊧̸φ⁡(1,2)\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}\not\models\varphi(1,2).
For d∈ℕd\in\mathbb{N} and a set A⊆ℝdA\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}, we say that AA is definable (in ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}) if there exist m∈ℕm\in\mathbb{N}, a formula φ⁡(v1,…,vd,w1,…,wm)\varphi(v_{1},\ldots,v_{d},w_{1},\ldots,w_{m}) in ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi} and b∈ℝmb\in\mathbb{R}^{m} such that A={a∈ℝd:ℝexp,Φ⊧φ⁡(a,b)}A=\{a\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}\models\varphi(a,b)\}; we will sometimes abbreviate the notation and write A={a∈ℝd:φ⁡(a,b)}A=\{a\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\varphi(a,b)\} instead. For d1,d2∈ℕd_{1},d_{2}\in\mathbb{N} and a function f:ℝd1→ℝd2f:\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}, we say that ff is definable (in ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}) if its graph {(x,y)∈ℝd1×ℝd2:y=f⁡(x)}\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}:y=f(x)\} is a definable set in ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}. Moreover, for a collection 𝒞\mathcal{C} of subsets of ℝd\mathbb{R}^{d}, we say that 𝒞\mathcal{C} is uniformly definable (in ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}) if there exists a formula φ⁡(v,w)\varphi(v,w), where v=(v1,…,vd)v=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{d}) and w=(w1,…,wm)w=(w_{1},\ldots,w_{m}) for some m∈ℕm\in\mathbb{N}, such that for every C∈𝒞C\in\mathcal{C}, there exists bC∈ℝmb_{C}\in\mathbb{R}^{m} with C={a∈ℝd:φ⁡(a,bC)}C=\{a\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\varphi(a,b_{C})\}. Similarly, for a collection ℱ\mathcal{F} of functions from ℝd1\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}} to ℝd2\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}, we say that ℱ\mathcal{F} is uniformly definable (in ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}) if the collection of graphs of functions in ℱ\mathcal{F} is uniformly definable.
Lemma 14.
(a)
Let ℱ\mathcal{F} be a collection of uniformly definable functions from ℝd1\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}} to ℝ\mathbb{R}, and let 𝒢\mathcal{G} be a collection of uniformly definable functions from ℝd2\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}} to ℝ\mathbb{R}. Then {{(x,y)∈ℝd1×ℝ:y≤f⁡(x)}:f∈ℱ}\bigl\{\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}:y\leq f(x)\}:f\in\mathcal{F}\bigr\}, {(x,z)↦f(x)+g(z):f∈ℱ,g∈𝒢}\{(x,z)\mapsto f(x)+g(z):f\in\mathcal{F},g\in\mathcal{G}\} and {(x,z)↦f(x)⋅g(z):f∈ℱ,g∈𝒢}\{(x,z)\mapsto f(x)\cdot g(z):f\in\mathcal{F},g\in\mathcal{G}\} are all uniformly definable.
(b)
Let ℱ\mathcal{F} be a collection of uniformly definable functions from ℝd\mathbb{R}^{d} to ℝ∖{0}\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}. Then {x↦1/f⁡(x):f∈ℱ}\{x\mapsto 1/f(x):f\in\mathcal{F}\} is uniformly definable.
(c)
Let p∈ℕp\in\mathbb{N} and for j∈[p]j\in[p], let 𝒢j\mathcal{G}_{j} be a collection of uniformly definable functions from ℝdj\mathbb{R}^{d_{j}} to ℝ\mathbb{R} for some dj∈ℕd_{j}\in\mathbb{N}. Further let ℱ\mathcal{F} be a collection of uniformly definable functions from ℝp\mathbb{R}^{p} to ℝ\mathbb{R}. Then {(x1,…,xp)↦f(g1(x1),…,gp(xp)):f∈ℱ,gj∈𝒢j∀j∈[p]}\bigl\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{p})\mapsto f\bigl(g_{1}(x_{1}),\ldots,g_{p}(x_{p})\bigr):f\in\mathcal{F},g_{j}\in\mathcal{G}_{j}\,\forall j\in[p]\bigr\} is uniformly definable.
Proof.
(a) Suppose that for each f∈ℱf\in\mathcal{F}, the graph of ff is {(x,y)∈ℝd1×ℝ:φ⁡(x,y,bf)}\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}:\varphi(x,y,b_{f})\} for some formula φ\varphi, some bf∈ℝmb_{f}\in\mathbb{R}^{m} and some m∈ℕm\in\mathbb{N}; also suppose that for each g∈𝒢g\in\mathcal{G}, the graph of gg is {(z,y)∈ℝd2×ℝ:ψ⁡(z,y,cg)}\{(z,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}:\psi(z,y,c_{g})\} for some formula ψ\psi, some cg∈ℝnc_{g}\in\mathbb{R}^{n} and some n∈ℕn\in\mathbb{N}. The subgraph {(x,y)∈ℝd1×ℝ:y≤f⁡(x)}\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}:y\leq f(x)\} of f∈ℱf\in\mathcal{F} can be written as {(x,y)∈ℝd1×ℝ:∃t⁡(φ⁡(x,t,bf)∧(y<t∨y=t))}\bigl\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}:\exists t\;\bigl(\varphi(x,t,b_{f})\wedge(y<t\vee y=t)\bigr)\bigr\}. Hence the collection of subgraphs of f∈ℱf\in\mathcal{F} is uniformly definable. For f∈ℱf\in\mathcal{F} and g∈𝒢g\in\mathcal{G}, the graph of the function (x,z)↦f⁡(x)+g⁡(z)(x,z)\mapsto f(x)+g(z) can be written as {(x,z,y)∈ℝd1×ℝd2×ℝ:∃u,v(φ(x,u,bf)∧ψ(z,v,cg)∧(y=u+v))}\bigl\{(x,z,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}:\exists u,v\;\bigl(\varphi(x,u,b_{f})\wedge\psi(z,v,c_{g})\wedge(y=u+v)\bigr)\bigr\}. Finally, the graph of the function (x,z)↦f⁡(x)⋅g⁡(z)(x,z)\mapsto f(x)\cdot g(z) can be written as {(x,z,y)∈ℝd1×ℝd2×ℝ:∃u,v(φ(x,u,bf)∧ψ(z,v,cg)∧(y=u⋅v))}\bigl\{(x,z,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}:\exists u,v\;\bigl(\varphi(x,u,b_{f})\wedge\psi(z,v,c_{g})\wedge(y=u\cdot v)\bigr)\bigr\}.
(b) Again, suppose that for each f∈ℱf\in\mathcal{F}, the graph of ff is {(x,y)∈ℝd×ℝ:φ⁡(x,y,bf)}\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}:\varphi(x,y,b_{f})\} for some formula φ\varphi, some bf∈ℝmb_{f}\in\mathbb{R}^{m} and some m∈ℕm\in\mathbb{N}. Then, for f∈ℱf\in\mathcal{F}, the graph of the function x↦1/f⁡(x)x\mapsto 1/f(x) can be written as {(x,y)∈ℝd×ℝ:∃u⁡(φ⁡(x,u,bf)∧(y⋅u=1))}\bigl\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}:\exists u\;\bigl(\varphi(x,u,b_{f})\wedge(y\cdot u=1)\bigr)\bigr\}. This proves the claim.
(c) Suppose that for each f∈ℱf\in\mathcal{F}, the graph of ff is {(x,y)∈ℝp×ℝ:φ⁡(x,y,bf)}\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{p}\times\mathbb{R}:\varphi(x,y,b_{f})\} for some formula φ\varphi, some bf∈ℝmb_{f}\in\mathbb{R}^{m} and some m∈ℕm\in\mathbb{N}; also suppose that for j∈[p]j\in[p] and gj∈𝒢jg_{j}\in\mathcal{G}_{j}, the graph of gjg_{j} is {(z,y)∈ℝdj×ℝ:ψj​(z,y,cgj)}\{(z,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{j}}\times\mathbb{R}:\psi_{j}(z,y,c_{g_{j}})\} for some formula ψj\psi_{j}, some cgj∈ℝnjc_{g_{j}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{j}} and some nj∈ℕn_{j}\in\mathbb{N}. Then the graph of the function (x1,…,xp)↦f⁡(g1​(x1),…,gp​(xp))(x_{1},\ldots,x_{p})\mapsto f\bigl(g_{1}(x_{1}),\ldots,g_{p}(x_{p})\bigr) can be written as
{(x1,…,xp,y)∈ℝp+1:∃v1,…,vp(⋀j=1pψj(xj,vj,cgj)∧φ(v1,…,vp,y,bf))}.\displaystyle\biggl\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{p},y)\in\mathbb{R}^{p+1}:\exists v_{1},\ldots,v_{p}\biggl(\bigwedge_{j=1}^{p}\psi_{j}(x_{j},v_{j},c_{g_{j}})\wedge\varphi(v_{1},\ldots,v_{p},y,b_{f})\biggr)\biggr\}.
This proves the claim.
∎
Lemma 15.
For any L,H,dmodel,dffn∈ℕL,H,{d_{\mathrm{model}}},d_{\mathrm{ffn}}\in\mathbb{N}, the class ℱ′≔𝖱𝖾𝖺𝖽∘ℱTF​(L,H,dmodel,dffn)\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathsf{Read}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{TF}}(L,H,{d_{\mathrm{model}}},d_{\mathrm{ffn}}) has finite pseudo-dimension.
Remark.
It is possible to obtain upper bounds on the pseudo-dimension of ℱ′\mathcal{F}^{\prime} with polynomial dependence on LL, HH, dmodel{d_{\mathrm{model}}} and dffnd_{\mathrm{ffn}} via a more detailed analysis, but a finite pseudo-dimension is already enough for our purpose.
Proof.
Note that ℱ′\mathcal{F}^{\prime} is defined on the space ⋃n=1Nℝ(n+1)×dmodel\bigcup_{n=1}^{N}\mathbb{R}^{(n+1)\times{d_{\mathrm{model}}}}, equipped with the disjoint union topology. Thus, it suffices to show that, for each n∈[N]n\in[N], the restriction ℱn′\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{n} of the functions in ℱ′\mathcal{F}^{\prime} to ℝ(n+1)×dmodel\mathbb{R}^{(n+1)\times{d_{\mathrm{model}}}} has finite pseudo-dimension.
Now let
𝒞n≔{{(Z,y)∈ℝ(n+1)×dmodel×ℝ:y≤f⁡(Z)}:f∈ℱn′}\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{n}\coloneqq\Bigl\{\bigl\{(Z,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{(n+1)\times{d_{\mathrm{model}}}}\times\mathbb{R}:y\leq f(Z)\bigr\}:f\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{n}\Bigr\}
denote the collection of subgraphs of f∈ℱn′f\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{n}. Then by definition, the pseudo-dimension of ℱn′\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{n} is equal to the VC-dimension of 𝒞n\mathcal{C}_{n}. We will now show that 𝒞n\mathcal{C}_{n} is uniformly definable in ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}. The graph {(x,y)∈ℝ2:y=𝖱𝖾𝖫𝖴⁡(x)}\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:y=\relu(x)\} of 𝖱𝖾𝖫𝖴\relu can be written as
{(x,y)∈ℝ2:(¬(x<0)∧y=x)∨(x<0∧y=0)},\displaystyle\bigl\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:\bigl(\neg(x<0)\wedge y=x\bigr)\vee(x<0\wedge y=0)\bigr\},
so 𝖱𝖾𝖫𝖴:ℝ→ℝ\relu:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R} is definable on ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}. By Lemma 14, sums, products, reciprocals and compositions of definable functions are definable. Thus, for any d,p∈ℕd,p\in\mathbb{N}, the set of polynomials on ℝd\mathbb{R}^{d} with degree at most pp is uniformly definable in ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}. Moreover, since exp\exp and Φ\Phi are definable in ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi} by definition, we deduce that 𝖦𝖤𝖫𝖴\mathsf{GELU}, 𝖲𝗂𝖫𝖴\mathsf{SiLU}, and 𝖲𝗈𝖿𝗍𝗆𝖺𝗑\softmax are also definable in ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}. Each function f∈ℱn′f\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{n} comprises compositions, sums and products of ρ∈{𝖱𝖾𝖫𝖴,𝖦𝖤𝖫𝖴,𝖲𝗂𝖫𝖴}\rho\in\{\mathsf{ReLU},\mathsf{GELU},\mathsf{SiLU}\}, 𝖲𝗈𝖿𝗍𝗆𝖺𝗑\softmax and polynomials of degree at most two, we deduce by Lemma 14 that ℱn′\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{n} is uniformly definable in ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}. Further, by Lemma 14(a), we have that the subgraph class of ℱn′\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{n} is also uniformly definable on ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi} and
𝒞n⊆{{a∈ℝ(n+1)×dmodel×ℝ:ℝexp,Φ⊧φ⁡(a,b)}:b∈ℝm}.\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{n}\subseteq\Bigl\{\bigl\{a\in\mathbb{R}^{(n+1)\times{d_{\mathrm{model}}}}\times\mathbb{R}:\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi}\models\varphi(a,b)\bigr\}:b\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\Bigr\}.
By Marker (2002, Therorem 3.4.37), the structure ℝexp≔(ℝ;0,1,+,−,⋅,<,exp,Φ)\mathbb{R}_{\exp}\coloneqq(\mathbb{R};0,1,+,-,\cdot,<,\exp,\Phi) is o-minimal, i.e. every definable set is a finite union of open intervals and singleton points. Further, the derivative Φ′\Phi^{\prime} given by Φ′(x)=12​πe−x2/2\Phi^{\prime}(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-x^{2}/2} is definable in ℝexp\mathbb{R}_{\exp}, so by Speissegger (1999), there exists an o-minimal extension ℝ~\widetilde{\mathbb{R}} of ℝexp\mathbb{R}_{\exp} in which Φ\Phi is definable. Now, every definable set in ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi} is definable in ℝ~\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}, so ℝexp,Φ\mathbb{R}_{\exp,\Phi} is o-minimal.
We may therefore apply Laskowski (1992, Theorem 3.2) (see also Karpinski and Macintyre, 1995, Theorem 2) to deduce that the VC-dimension of 𝒞n\mathcal{C}_{n} is finite.
∎
Proof of Proposition 3.
By Theorem 2, there exist dmodel∘≥d+2d_{\mathrm{model}}^{\circ}\geq d+2 and dffn∘∈ℕd_{\mathrm{ffn}}^{\circ}\in\mathbb{N} such that if dmodel≥dmodel∘{d_{\mathrm{model}}}\geq d_{\mathrm{model}}^{\circ}, dffn≥dffn∘d_{\mathrm{ffn}}\geq d_{\mathrm{ffn}}^{\circ} and ℱ≔𝖱𝖾𝖺𝖽∘ℱTF​(3,1,dmodel,dffn)\mathcal{F}\coloneqq\mathsf{Read}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{TF}}(3,1,{d_{\mathrm{model}}},d_{\mathrm{ffn}}), then
inff∈ℱ𝔼P∼π,(𝒟n,X,Y)|P∼P⊗(n+1)​{(f⁡(X,𝒟n)−gπ​(X,𝒟n))2}≤ϵ4,\displaystyle\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}_{P\sim\pi,\,(\mathcal{D}_{n},X,Y)|P\sim P^{\otimes(n+1)}}\Bigl\{\bigl(f(X,\mathcal{D}_{n})-g_{\pi}(X,\mathcal{D}_{n})\bigr)^{2}\Bigr\}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4},
Now by Lemma 15, we have that Pdim⁡(ℱ)\mathrm{Pdim}(\mathcal{F}) is finite. Thus, for all TT sufficiently large, we have C​log3⁡(T)⋅Pdim⁡(ℱ)T≤ϵ2\frac{C\log^{3}(T)\cdot\mathrm{Pdim}(\mathcal{F})}{T}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}, where C>0C>0 is taken from Lemma 13. The claim then follows from Lemma 13.
∎
Appendix D Function spaces and wavelets
D.1 Function spaces
In this section, we restrict the domain of the functions to [0,1]d[0,1]^{d}. By Jensen’s inequality, ‖f‖Lp1≤‖f‖Lp2\|f\|_{L^{p_{1}}}\leq\|f\|_{L^{p_{2}}} for all 1≤p1≤p2≤∞1\leq p_{1}\leq p_{2}\leq\infty.
For f,g∈L2​([0,1]d)f,g\in L^{2}([0,1]^{d}), define the inner product
⟨f,g⟩≔∫[0,1]df⁡(x)​g​(x)​𝑑x.\displaystyle\langle f,g\rangle\coloneqq\int_{[0,1]^{d}}f(x)g(x)\,\mathrm{d}x.
Let C⁡([0,1]d)C([0,1]^{d}) denote the set of continuous functions from [0,1]d[0,1]^{d} to ℝ\mathbb{R}. For m=(m1,…,md)⊤∈ℕ0dm=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{d})^{\top}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d} with k≔‖m‖1k\coloneqq\|m\|_{1}, and a kk-times differentiable f:[0,1]d→ℝf:[0,1]^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}, we use the multi-index notation ∂mf≔∂m1⋯∂mdf\partial^{m}f\coloneqq\partial^{m_{1}}\cdots\partial^{m_{d}}f for partial derivatives.
Definition 6 (Hölder space).
For α>0\alpha>0, let α0≔⌈α⌉−1\alpha_{0}\coloneqq\lceil\alpha\rceil-1 be the largest integer strictly smaller than α\alpha and define the Hölder space Hα​([0,1]d)H^{\alpha}([0,1]^{d}) to be the set of α0\alpha_{0}-times differentiable f:[0,1]d→ℝf:[0,1]^{d}\to\mathbb{R} with
supx≠y∑m∈ℕ0d:‖m‖1=α0∂mf⁡(x)−∂mf⁡(y)‖x−y‖2α−α0<∞.\displaystyle\sup_{x\neq y}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}:\|m\|_{1}=\alpha_{0}}\frac{\partial^{m}f(x)-\partial^{m}f(y)}{\|x-y\|_{2}^{\alpha-\alpha_{0}}}<\infty.
For r∈ℕr\in\mathbb{N}, define the rrth order difference operator Δhr:L1​([0,1]d)→L1​([0,1]d)\Delta_{h}^{r}:L^{1}([0,1]^{d})\rightarrow L^{1}([0,1]^{d}) by
Δhr(f)(x)=𝟙{x+rh∈[0,1]d}∑k=0r(rk)(−1)r−kf(x+kh).\displaystyle\Delta_{h}^{r}(f)(x)=\mathbbm{1}_{\{x+rh\in[0,1]^{d}\}}\sum_{k=0}^{r}\binom{r}{k}(-1)^{r-k}f(x+kh).
Note that Δh1(f)(x)={f(x+h)−f(x)}𝟙{x+h∈[0,1]d}\Delta_{h}^{1}(f)(x)=\{f(x+h)-f(x)\}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x+h\in[0,1]^{d}\}} and Δhr​(f)​(x)=Δh1​(Δhr−1​(f)​(x))\Delta_{h}^{r}(f)(x)=\Delta_{h}^{1}\bigl(\Delta_{h}^{r-1}(f)(x)\bigr) if x+r​h∈[0,1]dx+rh\in[0,1]^{d}. Now, for p∈[1,∞]p\in[1,\infty], f∈Lp​([0,1]d)f\in L^{p}([0,1]^{d}) and t≥0t\geq 0, define the rrth modulus of smoothness
ωr,p​(f,t)≔sup‖h‖2≤t‖Δhr​(f)‖Lp.\displaystyle\omega_{r,p}(f,t)\coloneqq\sup_{\|h\|_{2}\leq t}\|\Delta_{h}^{r}(f)\|_{L^{p}}.
Definition 7 (Besov space).
For p,q∈[1,∞]p,q\in[1,\infty] and α>0\alpha>0, let r≔⌊α⌋+1r\coloneqq\lfloor\alpha\rfloor+1 and define the Besov space Bp,qα​([0,1]d)B_{p,q}^{\alpha}([0,1]^{d}) by
Bp,qα([0,1]d)≔{{f∈Lp​([0,1]d):[∫0∞(t−α​ωr,p​(f,t))q​d​tt]1/q<∞}for ​q∈[1,∞){f∈Lp​([0,1]d):supt>0t−α​ωr,p​(f,t)<∞}for ​q=∞.\displaystyle B_{p,q}^{\alpha}([0,1]^{d})\coloneqq\begin{cases}{\displaystyle\biggl\{f\in L^{p}([0,1]^{d}):\biggl[\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigl(t^{-\alpha}\omega_{r,p}(f,t)\bigr)^{q}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\biggr]^{1/q}<\infty\biggr\}}\quad&\text{for }q\in[1,\infty)\\[10.00002pt]
{\displaystyle\Bigl\{f\in L^{p}([0,1]^{d}):\sup_{t>0}t^{-\alpha}\omega_{r,p}(f,t)<\infty\Bigr\}}\quad&\text{for }q=\infty.\end{cases}
For non-integer smoothness levels, Hölder spaces are special cases of Besov spaces in the sense that Hα​([0,1]d)=B∞,∞α​([0,1]d)H^{\alpha}([0,1]^{d})=B^{\alpha}_{\infty,\infty}([0,1]^{d}) for all α∈(0,∞)∖ℕ\alpha\in(0,\infty)\setminus\mathbb{N}, while for α∈ℕ\alpha\in\mathbb{N}, we have Hα​([0,1]d)⊆B∞,∞α​([0,1]d)H^{\alpha}([0,1]^{d})\subseteq B^{\alpha}_{\infty,\infty}([0,1]^{d}), and in fact, B∞,∞α​([0,1]d)B_{\infty,\infty}^{\alpha}([0,1]^{d}) coincides with Zygmund spaces when α∈ℕ\alpha\in\mathbb{N}; see e.g. Giné and Nickl (2021, p. 351) or Triebel (1983, Chapter 2.5.7).
D.2 Wavelets
We start with the univariate setting where the notation is simpler. For S∈ℕS\in\mathbb{N}, denote the Cohen–Daubechies–Vial (CDV) wavelet basis on [0,1][0,1] (Cohen, Daubechies and Vial, 1993), constructed from SS-regular and SS-times continuously differentiable father and mother Daubechies wavelets (Daubechies, 1988), by
{ϕk:k∈[0:2ℓ0−1]}⋃{ψℓ,k:ℓ∈[ℓ0:∞),k∈[0:2ℓ−1]},\displaystyle\bigl\{\phi_{k}:k\in[0:2^{\ell_{0}}-1]\bigr\}\bigcup\bigl\{\psi_{\ell,k}:\ell\in[{\ell_{0}}:\infty),\,k\in[0:2^{\ell}-1]\bigr\},
(27)
where ℓ0∈ℕ\ell_{0}\in\mathbb{N} depends only on SS. The precise definition and construction of this wavelet basis is not important for us, but the functions in (27) are SS-times continuously differentiable and form an orthonormal basis for L2​([0,1])L^{2}([0,1]) (Cohen, Daubechies and Vial, 1993, Theorem 4.4). Moreover, ϕk\phi_{k} is supported on [k2ℓ0,k+12ℓ0]\bigl[\frac{k}{2^{\ell_{0}}},\frac{k+1}{2^{\ell_{0}}}\bigr] for k∈[0:2ℓ0−1]k\in[0:2^{\ell_{0}}-1], and ψℓ,k\psi_{\ell,k} is supported on [k2ℓ,k+12ℓ]\bigl[\frac{k}{2^{\ell}},\frac{k+1}{2^{\ell}}\bigr] for ℓ∈[ℓ0:∞),k∈[0:2ℓ−1]\ell\in[{\ell_{0}}:\infty),\,k\in[0:2^{\ell}-1].
The one-dimensional CDV wavelets in (27) can be extended to an orthonormal basis for L2​([0,1]d)L^{2}([0,1]^{d}) via tensor products. For k=(k1,…,kd)⊤∈[0:2ℓ0−1]dk=(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})^{\top}\in[0:2^{\ell_{0}}-1]^{d} and x=(x1,…,xd)⊤∈[0,1]dx=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})^{\top}\in[0,1]^{d}, define
Φk​(x)≔∏j=1dϕkj​(xj).\displaystyle\Phi_{k}(x)\coloneqq\prod_{j=1}^{d}\phi_{k_{j}}(x_{j}).
(28)
Further, for τ=(τ1,…,τd)⊤∈{0,1}d∖{𝟎d}\tau=(\tau_{1},\ldots,\tau_{d})^{\top}\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\bm{0}_{d}\}, ℓ∈[ℓ0:∞)\ell\in[{\ell_{0}}:\infty), k=(k1,…,kd)⊤∈[0:2ℓ−1]dk=(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})^{\top}\in[0:2^{\ell}-1]^{d} and x=(x1,…,xd)⊤∈[0,1]dx=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})^{\top}\in[0,1]^{d}, define
Ψℓ,k(τ)​(x)≔∏j=1dψℓ,kj(τj)​(xj),\displaystyle\Psi_{\ell,k}^{(\tau)}(x)\coloneqq\prod_{j=1}^{d}\psi^{(\tau_{j})}_{\ell,k_{j}}(x_{j}),
(29)
where ψℓ,k′(1)=ψℓ,k′\psi^{(1)}_{\ell,k^{\prime}}=\psi_{\ell,k^{\prime}} for all k′∈[0:2ℓ−1]k^{\prime}\in[0:2^{\ell}-1], and (ψℓ,k′(0))k′=02ℓ−1(\psi^{(0)}_{\ell,k^{\prime}})_{k^{\prime}=0}^{2^{\ell}-1} are the boundary-corrected (SS-regular and SS-times continuously differentiable) father Daubechies wavelets at resolution ℓ\ell. Again, the precise definition and construction are not important for us, but we will exploit the following properties. To simplify notation, define
K≔[0:2ℓ0−1]dandΓℓ≔[0:2ℓ−1]d×({0,1}d∖{𝟎d}) for ℓ∈[ℓ0:∞),\displaystyle K\coloneqq[0:2^{\ell_{0}}-1]^{d}\quad\text{and}\quad\Gamma_{\ell}\coloneqq[0:2^{\ell}-1]^{d}\times\bigl(\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\bm{0}_{d}\}\bigr)\text{ for }\ell\in[\ell_{0}:\infty),
(30)
and for γ=(k,τ)∈Γℓ\gamma=(k,\tau)\in\Gamma_{\ell}, we write Ψℓ,γ≔Ψℓ,k(τ)\Psi_{\ell,\gamma}\coloneqq\Psi_{\ell,k}^{(\tau)}. Note that
|K|=2ℓ0​dand|Γℓ|=2ℓ​d(2d−1)<2(ℓ+1)​d for ℓ∈[ℓ0:∞).\displaystyle|K|=2^{\ell_{0}d}\quad\text{and}\quad|\Gamma_{\ell}|=2^{\ell d}(2^{d}-1)<2^{(\ell+1)d}\text{ for }\ell\in[\ell_{0}:\infty).
(31)
Each term in the products in (28) and (29) is SS-times continuously differentiable, and the functions
{Φk:k∈K}∪{Ψℓ,γ:ℓ∈[ℓ0:∞),γ∈Γℓ}\displaystyle\bigl\{\Phi_{k}:k\in K\bigr\}\cup\bigl\{\Psi_{\ell,\gamma}:\ell\in[{\ell_{0}}:\infty),\,\gamma\in\Gamma_{\ell}\bigr\}
form an orthonormal basis for L2​([0,1]d)L^{2}([0,1]^{d}); see e.g. Giné and Nickl (2021, Chapter 4.3.6) and Dahmen (1997, Section 4). By construction, there exist C,C′>0C,C^{\prime}>0 (depending only on SS and dd) such that
‖Φk‖∞≤C​2ℓ0​d/2and‖Ψℓ,γ‖∞≤C​2ℓ​d/2\displaystyle\|\Phi_{k}\|_{\infty}\leq C2^{\ell_{0}d/2}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\Psi_{\ell,\gamma}\|_{\infty}\leq C2^{\ell d/2}
(32)
for k∈Kk\in K, ℓ∈[ℓ0:∞)\ell\in[\ell_{0}:\infty) and γ∈Γℓ\gamma\in\Gamma_{\ell}, and that
∑k∈K𝟙{Φk(x)≠0}∨supℓ∈[ℓ0:∞)∑γ∈Γℓ𝟙{Ψℓ,γ(x)≠0}≤C′for all x∈[0,1]d.\displaystyle\sum_{k\in K}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Phi_{k}(x)\neq 0\}}\vee\sup_{\ell\in[\ell_{0}:\infty)}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{\ell}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Psi_{\ell,\gamma}(x)\neq 0\}}\leq C^{\prime}\quad\text{for all }x\in[0,1]^{d}.
(33)
For f∈L2​([0,1]d)f\in L^{2}([0,1]^{d}), we have the wavelet decomposition
f=∑k∈Kak​Φk+∑ℓ=ℓ0∞∑γ∈Γℓbℓ,γ​Ψℓ,γ,\displaystyle f=\sum_{k\in K}a_{k}\Phi_{k}+\sum_{\ell=\ell_{0}}^{\infty}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{\ell}}b_{\ell,\gamma}\Psi_{\ell,\gamma},
(34)
where ak≔⟨f,Φk⟩a_{k}\coloneqq\langle f,\Phi_{k}\rangle and bℓ,γ≔⟨f,Ψℓ,γ⟩b_{\ell,\gamma}\coloneqq\langle f,\Psi_{\ell,\gamma}\rangle. Define
a≔(ak)k∈K∈ℝKandbℓ≔(bℓ,γ)γ∈Γℓ∈ℝΓℓ for ℓ∈[ℓ0:∞).\displaystyle a\coloneqq(a_{k})_{k\in K}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}\quad\text{and}\quad b_{\ell}\coloneqq(b_{\ell,\gamma})_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{\ell}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\Gamma_{\ell}}\text{ for }\ell\in[\ell_{0}:\infty).
(35)
For f∈L2​([0,1]d)f\in L^{2}([0,1]^{d}), p∈[2,∞]p\in[2,\infty], q∈[1,∞]q\in[1,\infty] and α∈(0,S)\alpha\in(0,S), define its Besov norm by
∥f∥Bp,qα≔{2ℓ0​(d/2−d/p)​‖a‖p+{∑ℓ=ℓ0∞(2ℓ⁡(α+d/2−d/p)​‖bℓ‖p)q}1/qfor ​q∈[1,∞)2ℓ0​(d/2−d/p)∥a∥p+supℓ∈[ℓ0:∞)2ℓ⁡(α+d/2−d/p)∥bℓ∥pfor ​q=∞.\displaystyle\|f\|_{B_{p,q}^{\alpha}}\coloneqq\begin{cases}\displaystyle 2^{\ell_{0}(d/2-d/p)}\|a\|_{p}+\biggl\{\sum_{\ell={\ell_{0}}}^{\infty}\bigl(2^{\ell(\alpha+d/2-d/p)}\|b_{\ell}\|_{p}\bigr)^{q}\biggr\}^{1/q}\quad&\text{for }q\in[1,\infty)\\
\displaystyle 2^{\ell_{0}(d/2-d/p)}\|a\|_{p}+\sup_{\ell\in[\ell_{0}:\infty)}2^{\ell(\alpha+d/2-d/p)}\|b_{\ell}\|_{p}\quad&\text{for }q=\infty.\end{cases}
For p∈[2,∞]p\in[2,\infty], q∈[1,∞]q\in[1,\infty] and α∈(0,S)\alpha\in(0,S), the Besov space Bp,qα​([0,1]d)B_{p,q}^{\alpha}([0,1]^{d}) in Definition 7 can be equivalently characterised by wavelet coefficients in the sense that f∈Bp,qα​([0,1]d)f\in B_{p,q}^{\alpha}([0,1]^{d}) if and only if f∈L2​([0,1]d)f\in L^{2}([0,1]^{d}) and ‖f‖Bp,qα<∞\|f\|_{B_{p,q}^{\alpha}}<\infty (e.g. Giné and Nickl, 2021, Chapter 4.3.6). Therefore, we can define a closed ball Bp,qα​([0,1]d,R)B_{p,q}^{\alpha}([0,1]^{d},R) of radius R>0R>0 in the space Bp,qα​([0,1]d)B_{p,q}^{\alpha}([0,1]^{d}) by
Bp,qα​([0,1]d,R)≔{f∈L2​([0,1]d):‖f‖Bp,qα≤R}.\displaystyle B_{p,q}^{\alpha}([0,1]^{d},R)\coloneqq\{f\in L^{2}([0,1]^{d}):\|f\|_{B_{p,q}^{\alpha}}\leq R\}.
(36)
As a special case, we have ∥f∥B∞,∞α=2ℓ0​d/2∥a∥∞+supℓ∈[ℓ0:∞)2ℓ⁡(α+d/2)∥bℓ∥∞\|f\|_{B_{\infty,\infty}^{\alpha}}=2^{\ell_{0}d/2}\|a\|_{\infty}+\sup_{\ell\in[\ell_{0}:\infty)}2^{\ell(\alpha+d/2)}\|b_{\ell}\|_{\infty}.
Appendix E Posterior contraction and adaptation
E.1 General theory
This section is primarily based on material from Ghosal, Lember and van der Vaart (2008); see also Ghosal and van der Vaart (2017, Section 10.2). Here, we make some modifications in order to obtain finite-sample posterior contraction at an exponential rate.
Let 𝒵\mathcal{Z} be a measurable space and let 𝒜\mathcal{A} be a countable index set. For α∈𝒜\alpha\in\mathcal{A}, let 𝒫α\mathcal{P}_{\alpha} be a space of distributions on 𝒵\mathcal{Z} dominated by a σ\sigma-finite measure ν\nu, and let 𝒫≔⋃α∈𝒜𝒫α\mathcal{P}\coloneqq\bigcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{P}_{\alpha}, which is a metric space when equipped with the Hellinger metric dHd_{\mathrm{H}}. For example, α>0\alpha>0 might index smoothness, in which case 𝒫α\mathcal{P}_{\alpha} might denote the set of distributions of (X,Y)(X,Y) for which the regression function x↦𝔼⁡(Y|X=x)x\mapsto\mathbb{E}(Y\,|\,X=x) is α\alpha-smooth. For α∈𝒜\alpha\in\mathcal{A}, let πα\pi_{\alpha} be a distribution on 𝒫α\mathcal{P}_{\alpha} and let λ=(λα)α∈𝒜\lambda=(\lambda_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}} be such that λα>0\lambda_{\alpha}>0 for α∈𝒜\alpha\in\mathcal{A} and ∑α∈𝒜λα=1\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\lambda_{\alpha}=1. We consider a prior distribution π\pi on 𝒫\mathcal{P} of the form
π≔∑α∈𝒜λα​πα.\displaystyle\pi\coloneqq\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\lambda_{\alpha}\pi_{\alpha}.
For z1,…,zn∈𝒵z_{1},\ldots,z_{n}\in\mathcal{Z}, we may define a distribution on 𝒫\mathcal{P} by
π⁡(B|z1,…,zn)≔∫B∏i=1nd​Pd​ν​(zi)​𝑑π​(P)∫𝒫∏i=1nd​Pd​ν​(zi)​𝑑π​(P),\displaystyle\pi(B\,|\,z_{1},\ldots,z_{n})\coloneqq\frac{\int_{B}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\mathrm{d}P}{\mathrm{d}\nu}(z_{i})\,\mathrm{d}\pi(P)}{\int_{\mathcal{P}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\mathrm{d}P}{\mathrm{d}\nu}(z_{i})\,\mathrm{d}\pi(P)},
for any measurable B⊆𝒫B\subseteq\mathcal{P}. Thus, π(⋅|Z1,…,Zn)\pi(\cdot\,|\,Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}) would be the posterior distribution when PP has prior distribution π\pi and Z1,…,Zn|P∼iidPZ_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\,|\,P\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{iid}}}{{\sim}}P. In the sequel, however, we will assume instead that Z1,…,Zn∼iidP0Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{iid}}}{{\sim}}P_{0} for some fixed distribution P0P_{0} on 𝒵\mathcal{Z}, and seek bounds on the expected mass assigned by π(⋅|Z1,…,Zn)\pi(\cdot\,|\,Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}) to (Hellinger) balls around P0P_{0}.
For α∈𝒜\alpha\in\mathcal{A}, a distribution P0P_{0} on 𝒵\mathcal{Z} dominated by ν\nu, and for ϵ>0\epsilon>0, define
Bα(P0,ϵ)≔{P∈𝒫α:KL(P0,P)≤ϵ2,V2(P0,P)≤ϵ2}\displaystyle B_{\alpha}(P_{0},\epsilon)\coloneqq\Bigl\{P\in\mathcal{P}_{\alpha}:\mathrm{KL}(P_{0},P)\leq\epsilon^{2},\,\mathrm{V}_{2}(P_{0},P)\leq\epsilon^{2}\Bigr\}
(37)
and
Bα′​(P0,ϵ)≔{P∈𝒫α:dH​(P0,P)≤ϵ}.\displaystyle B^{\prime}_{\alpha}(P_{0},\epsilon)\coloneqq\Bigl\{P\in\mathcal{P}_{\alpha}:d_{\mathrm{H}}(P_{0},P)\leq\epsilon\Bigr\}.
(38)
Let (ϵn,α)α∈𝒜(\epsilon_{n,\alpha})_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}} be a positive sequence, thought of as the rate of convergence in Hellinger distance on each 𝒫α\mathcal{P}_{\alpha}. Fix β∈𝒜\beta\in\mathcal{A} and H≥1H\geq 1, and define
𝒜≥β\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\geq\beta}
≔{α∈𝒜:ϵn,α2≤H​ϵn,β2}\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigl\{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}:\epsilon_{n,\alpha}^{2}\leq H\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}\bigr\}
𝒜<β\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{<\beta}
≔{α∈𝒜:ϵn,α2>H​ϵn,β2},\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigl\{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}:\epsilon_{n,\alpha}^{2}>H\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}\bigr\},
to be the sets of indices that are ‘at least as regular as β\beta’ and ‘less regular than β\beta’, respectively. Here β\beta should be thought of as the index of the ‘best model’ for P0P_{0}.
We now state five conditions for our posterior contraction theory:
(C1)
Covering number bound: There exists a positive sequence (Eα)α∈𝒜(E_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}} such that
supϵ≥ϵn,αlog⁡𝒩⁡(ϵ/3,Bα′​(P0,2​ϵ),dH)≤Eα​n​ϵn,α2for all ​α∈𝒜.\displaystyle\sup_{\epsilon\geq\epsilon_{n,\alpha}}\log\mathcal{N}\bigl(\epsilon/3,\,B^{\prime}_{\alpha}(P_{0},2\epsilon),\,d_{\mathrm{H}}\bigr)\leq E_{\alpha}n\epsilon_{n,\alpha}^{2}\quad\text{for all }\alpha\in\mathcal{A}.
(C2)
Prior mass lower bound for β\beta: There exists F>0F>0 such that
πβ​(Bβ​(P0,ϵn,β))≥e−F​n​ϵn,β2.\displaystyle\pi_{\beta}\bigl(B_{\beta}(P_{0},\epsilon_{n,\beta})\bigr)\geq e^{-Fn\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}}.
(C3)
Mixture weights: There exists a positive sequence (μn,α)α∈𝒜(\mu_{n,\alpha})_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}} such that
λαλβ≤μn,α​en⁡(H−1​ϵn,α2∨ϵn,β2)for all ​α∈𝒜.\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{\alpha}}{\lambda_{\beta}}\leq\mu_{n,\alpha}\,e^{n(H^{-1}\epsilon_{n,\alpha}^{2}\vee\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2})}\quad\text{for all }\alpha\in\mathcal{A}.
(C4)
Prior mass upper bound for 𝒜<β\mathcal{A}_{<\beta}: There exist M,G>0M,G>0 such that
∑α∈𝒜<βλαλβ⋅πα​(Bα′​(P0,M​ϵn,α))≤G​e−(F+3)​n​ϵn,β2.\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}_{<\beta}}\frac{\lambda_{\alpha}}{\lambda_{\beta}}\cdot\pi_{\alpha}\bigl(B^{\prime}_{\alpha}(P_{0},M\epsilon_{n,\alpha})\bigr)\leq Ge^{-(F+3)n\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}}.
(C5)
Uniformity of covering number constants: There exists E∈(0,∞)E\in(0,\infty) such that
E≥supα∈𝒜<βEα∨supα∈𝒜≥βEα​ϵn,α2ϵn,β2.\displaystyle E\geq\sup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}_{<\beta}}E_{\alpha}\vee\sup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}_{\geq\beta}}\frac{E_{\alpha}\epsilon_{n,\alpha}^{2}}{\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}}.
The set 𝒜<β\mathcal{A}_{<\beta} may be empty; in that case, condition (C4) is automatically satisfied, and the supremum over the empty set in (C5) is defined as −∞-\infty.
The following theorem is a modification of Ghosal, Lember and van der Vaart (2008, Theorem 2.1), where we use a slightly larger constant in the exponent in (C4) and impose an additional sub-Gamma assumption on the log-likelihood ratios in order to obtain exponential posterior contraction (as opposed to the weaker conclusion that the left-hand side of (39) converges to zero as n→∞n\to\infty). In our in-context learning problem, this is crucial to derive rates of convergence for the posterior regression function. The sub-Gamma assumption is verified in Lemma 24(c) under a nonparametric regression setting with independent Gaussian noise.
Proposition 16.
Let P0P_{0} be a distribution on 𝒵\mathcal{Z}, let β∈𝒜\beta\in\mathcal{A} and let Z1,…,Zn​∼iid​P0Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\overset{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}P_{0}.
Assume that the conditions (C1)–(C5) hold with M2>243​(F+1)​(H​E+1)M^{2}>243(F+1)(HE+1) and ∑α∈𝒜μn,α≤en​ϵn,β2\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sqrt{\mu_{n,\alpha}}\leq e^{n\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}}. For i∈[n]i\in[n] and P∈𝒫βP\in\mathcal{P}_{\beta} with with P≪P0P\ll P_{0}, let
W⁡(P,Zi)≔log⁡d​Pd​P0​(Zi)−𝔼​log⁡d​Pd​P0​(Zi).W(P,Z_{i})\coloneqq\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P}{\mathrm{d}P_{0}}(Z_{i})-\mathbb{E}\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P}{\mathrm{d}P_{0}}(Z_{i}).
Assume that there exist a,c>0a,c>0 such that for all P∈Bβ​(P0,ϵn,β)P\in B_{\beta}(P_{0},\epsilon_{n,\beta}), we have that W⁡(P,Z1)W(P,Z_{1}) is sub-Gamma in the left tail with variance parameter a​ϵn,β2a\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2} and scale parameter cc. Then there exist C1,c2,c3>0C_{1},c_{2},c_{3}>0, not depending on nn, such that if n​ϵn,β2≥c3n\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}\geq c_{3}, then
𝔼(Z1,…,Zn)∼P0⊗n​[π⁡({P∈𝒫:dH​(P,P0)≥M​ϵn,β}|Z1,…,Zn)]≤C1​e−c2​n​ϵn,β2.\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n})\sim P_{0}^{\otimes n}}\bigl[\pi\bigl(\{P\in\mathcal{P}:d_{\mathrm{H}}(P,P_{0})\geq M\epsilon_{n,\beta}\}\,\big|\,Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\bigr)\bigr]\leq C_{1}e^{-c_{2}n\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}}.
(39)
Proof.
Our assumptions ensure that the conditions of Ghosal, Lember and van der Vaart (2008, Theorem 2.1) hold66
6
As we seek a finite-sample bound, we do not require their condition that n​ϵn,β2→∞n\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}\to\infty as n→∞n\to\infty. with K=1/9K=1/9, L=K/3=1/27L=K/3=1/27, I=27​(F+1)I=\sqrt{27(F+1)} and B=M/I=M/27​(F+1)B=M/I=M/\sqrt{27(F+1)} therein. Moreover, our conditions (C2) and (C4) ensure that
∑α∈𝒜<βλαλβ⋅πα​(Bα′​(P0,M​ϵn,α))πβ​(Bβ​(P0,ϵn,β))≤G​e−3​n​ϵn,β2.\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}_{<\beta}}\frac{\lambda_{\alpha}}{\lambda_{\beta}}\cdot\frac{\pi_{\alpha}\bigl(B^{\prime}_{\alpha}(P_{0},M\epsilon_{n,\alpha})\bigr)}{\pi_{\beta}\bigl(B_{\beta}(P_{0},\epsilon_{n,\beta})\bigr)}\leq Ge^{-3n\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}}.
(40)
Now, by following the proof of Ghosal, Lember and van der Vaart (2008, Theorem 2.1), replacing the use of their Eq. (2.4) by our (40), and the use of their Lemma 6.3 by our Lemma 23 (which applies because of our sub-Gamma assumption on W⁡(P,Z1)W(P,Z_{1})), we deduce the desired conclusion.
∎
E.2 Bayesian nonparametric regression
Let 𝒳⊆ℝd\mathcal{X}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d} and 𝒴=ℝ\mathcal{Y}=\mathbb{R}. In this section, we specialise to the setting of regression and take 𝒵=𝒳×𝒴\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}.
Let PXP_{X} be a Borel distribution on 𝒳\mathcal{X}, let X∼PXX\sim P_{X} and let ξ∼N⁡(0,σ2)\xi\sim N(0,\sigma^{2}) be independent of XX. Let R>0R>0, and for α∈𝒜\alpha\in\mathcal{A}, let 𝒢α\mathcal{G}_{\alpha} be a space of measurable functions from 𝒳\mathcal{X} to [−R,R][-R,R]. For any Borel measurable function g:𝒳→ℝg:\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{R}, we write PgP_{g} for the distribution of (X,Yg)(X,Y_{g}) where Yg=g⁡(X)+ξY_{g}=g(X)+\xi. For α∈𝒜\alpha\in\mathcal{A}, let π~α\widetilde{\pi}_{\alpha} be a distribution on 𝒢α\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}, let g~α∼π~α\widetilde{g}_{\alpha}\sim\widetilde{\pi}_{\alpha} and let πα\pi_{\alpha} denote the distribution of the random measure Pg~αP_{\widetilde{g}_{\alpha}}, where the randomness comes from g~α∼π~α\widetilde{g}_{\alpha}\sim\widetilde{\pi}_{\alpha}. Finally, let π~≔∑α∈𝒜λα​π~α\widetilde{\pi}\coloneqq\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\lambda_{\alpha}\widetilde{\pi}_{\alpha} and π≔∑α∈𝒜λα​πα\pi\coloneqq\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\lambda_{\alpha}\pi_{\alpha}, where λα≥0\lambda_{\alpha}\geq 0 for all α∈𝒜\alpha\in\mathcal{A} and ∑α∈𝒜λα=1\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\lambda_{\alpha}=1, let 𝒫\mathcal{P} be the support of π\pi and 𝒢≔⋃α∈𝒜𝒢α\mathcal{G}\coloneqq\bigcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}.
We first provide an alternative expression for the π\pi-posterior regression function gπg_{\pi}, defined in (4). For P∈𝒫P\in\mathcal{P}, let gP:𝒳→𝒴g_{P}:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y} be the regression function under PP, so that gP​(x)≔𝔼(X,Y)∼P​(Y|X=x)g_{P}(x)\coloneqq\mathbb{E}_{(X,Y)\sim P}(Y\,|\,X=x) for x∈𝒳x\in\mathcal{X}.
Then the π\pi-posterior regression function gπg_{\pi} satisfies
gπ​(x,Dn)\displaystyle g_{\pi}(x,D_{n})
=𝔼P∼π,(𝒟n,X,Y)|P∼P⊗(n+1){𝔼(Y|X=x,P)|X=x,𝒟n=Dn}\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P\sim\pi,\,(\mathcal{D}_{n},X,Y)|P\sim P^{\otimes(n+1)}}\bigl\{\mathbb{E}(Y\,|\,X=x,\,P)\,|\,X=x,\,\mathcal{D}_{n}=D_{n}\bigr\}
=𝔼P∼π,(𝒟n,X,Y)|P∼P⊗(n+1)​(gP​(x)|𝒟n=Dn)\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P\sim\pi,\,(\mathcal{D}_{n},X,Y)|P\sim P^{\otimes(n+1)}}\bigl(g_{P}(x)\,|\,\mathcal{D}_{n}=D_{n}\bigr)
=∫𝒫gP​(x)​𝑑π​(P|Dn)=∫𝒢g⁡(x)​𝑑π~​(g|Dn),\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{P}}g_{P}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\pi(P\,|\,D_{n})=\int_{\mathcal{G}}g(x)\,\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\pi}(g\,|\,D_{n}),
(41)
so gπ​(⋅,Dn)g_{\pi}(\cdot,D_{n}) can be thought of as the posterior mean of the regression function.
Corollary 17.
Let β∈𝒜\beta\in\mathcal{A}, let g0:𝒳→[−R,R]g^{0}:\mathcal{X}\to[-R,R] be measurable and write P0≡Pg0P_{0}\equiv P_{g^{0}}. Assume that the conditions (C1)–(C5) hold with M2>243​(F+1)​(H​E+1)M^{2}>243(F+1)(HE+1) and ∑α∈𝒜μn,α≤en​ϵn,β2\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sqrt{\mu_{n,\alpha}}\leq e^{n\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}}.
There exist C1,C2,c3,c4>0C_{1},C_{2},c_{3},c_{4}>0, not depending on nn, such that if n​ϵn,β2≥c4n\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}\geq c_{4}, then
𝔼(𝒟n,X,Y)∼P0⊗(n+1)​[{gπ​(X,𝒟n)−g0​(X)}2]≤C1​ϵn,β2+C2​e−c3​n​ϵn,β2.\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{(\mathcal{D}_{n},X,Y)\sim P_{0}^{\otimes(n+1)}}\bigl[\bigl\{g_{\pi}(X,\mathcal{D}_{n})-g^{0}(X)\bigr\}^{2}\bigr]\leq C_{1}\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}+C_{2}e^{-c_{3}n\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}}.
Proof.
We write 𝔼P0\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}} for 𝔼(𝒟n,X,Y)∼P0⊗(n+1)\mathbb{E}_{(\mathcal{D}_{n},X,Y)\sim P_{0}^{\otimes(n+1)}} and C1′≔2R2/(1−e−R2/(2σ2))C_{1}^{\prime}\coloneqq 2R^{2}/\bigl(1-e^{-R^{2}/(2\sigma^{2})}\bigr). We have by (41) that
𝔼P0[{gπ(X\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}\bigl[\bigl\{g_{\pi}(X
,𝒟n)−g0(X)}2]=𝔼P0[{∫𝒫gP(X)dπ(P|𝒟n)−g0(X)}2]\displaystyle,\mathcal{D}_{n})-g^{0}(X)\bigr\}^{2}\bigr]=\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}\biggl[\biggl\{\int_{\mathcal{P}}g_{P}(X)\,\mathrm{d}\pi(P\,|\,\mathcal{D}_{n})-g^{0}(X)\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]
≤𝔼P0​[∫𝒫{gP​(X)−g0​(X)}2​𝑑π​(P|𝒟n)]\displaystyle{\leq}\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}\biggl[\int_{\mathcal{P}}\bigl\{g_{P}(X)-g^{0}(X)\bigr\}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\pi(P\,|\,\mathcal{D}_{n})\biggr]
=𝔼𝒟n∼P0⊗n​(∫𝒫𝔼(X,Y)∼P0​[{gP​(X)−g0​(X)}2]​𝑑π​(P|𝒟n))\displaystyle{=}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}\sim P_{0}^{\otimes n}}\biggl(\int_{\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{(X,Y)\sim P_{0}}\bigl[\bigl\{g_{P}(X)-g^{0}(X)\bigr\}^{2}\bigr]\,\mathrm{d}\pi(P\,|\,\mathcal{D}_{n})\biggr)
≤C1′​𝔼𝒟n∼P0⊗n​[∫𝒫dH2​(P,P0)​𝑑π​(P|𝒟n)]\displaystyle{\leq}C_{1}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}\sim P_{0}^{\otimes n}}\biggl[\int_{\mathcal{P}}d_{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(P,P_{0})\,\mathrm{d}\pi(P\,|\,\mathcal{D}_{n})\biggr]
≤C1′​M2​ϵn,β2+2​C1′​𝔼𝒟n∼P0⊗n​[π⁡({P∈𝒫:dH​(P,P0)≥M​ϵn,β}|𝒟n)]\displaystyle{\leq}C_{1}^{\prime}M^{2}\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}+2C_{1}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}\sim P_{0}^{\otimes n}}\bigl[\pi\bigl(\{P\in\mathcal{P}:d_{\mathrm{H}}(P,P_{0})\geq M\epsilon_{n,\beta}\}\,\big|\,\mathcal{D}_{n}\bigr)\bigr]
≤C1​ϵn,β2+C2​e−c3​n​ϵn,β2.\displaystyle{\leq}C_{1}\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}+C_{2}e^{-c_{3}n\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}}.
Here, (i)(i) follows from Jensen’s inequality, (i​i)(ii) follows by Fubini’s theorem, (i​i​i)(iii) follows from Lemma 24(d), (i​v)(iv) follows by splitting the integral into regions where dH​(P,P0)<M​ϵn,βd_{\mathrm{H}}(P,P_{0})<M\epsilon_{n,\beta} and dH​(P,P0)≥M​ϵn,βd_{\mathrm{H}}(P,P_{0})\geq M\epsilon_{n,\beta} and (v)(v) follows from Proposition 16 where the sub-Gamma assumption is verified in Lemma 24(c).
∎
Appendix F Proofs for Section 4
F.1 Proof of Proposition 4
We begin with two preliminary lemmas in the setting of Section 4.1. Suppose that g∘∈𝒢βg^{\circ}\in\mathcal{G}_{\beta} has wavelet decomposition
g∘=∑k∈KC02−ℓ0d/2ak∘Φk+∑ℓ=ℓ0∞∑γ∈ΓℓC02−ℓ⁡(β+d/2)bℓ,γ∘Ψℓ,γ,\displaystyle g^{\circ}=\sum_{k\in K}C_{0}2^{-\ell_{0}d/2}a_{k}^{\circ}\Phi_{k}+\sum_{\ell=\ell_{0}}^{\infty}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{\ell}}C_{0}2^{-\ell(\beta+d/2)}b_{\ell,\gamma}^{\circ}\Psi_{\ell,\gamma},
(42)
where (ak∘)k∈K(a_{k}^{\circ})_{k\in K} and (bℓ,γ∘)ℓ∈[ℓ0:∞),γ∈Γℓ(b_{\ell,\gamma}^{\circ})_{\ell\in[\ell_{0}:\infty),\gamma\in\Gamma_{\ell}} are deterministic and satisfy |ak∘|≤1|a^{\circ}_{k}|\leq 1 and |bℓ,γ∘|≤1|b^{\circ}_{\ell,\gamma}|\leq 1 for k∈Kk\in K, ℓ∈[ℓ0:∞)\ell\in[\ell_{0}:\infty) and γ∈Γℓ\gamma\in\Gamma_{\ell}.
Lemma 18 below provides a lower bound on the prior mass; similar results for the one-dimensional case exist in the literature, e.g. Giné and Nickl (2011, Proposition 1) and Reiß and Schmidt-Hieber (2020, Lemma 4.1).
Lemma 18.
Let n∈ℕn\in\mathbb{N} and assume that (42) holds. Then there exists C>0C>0, depending only on β,d,c0,C0\beta,d,c_{0},C_{0} and SS, such that
π~β({g∈𝒢β:∥g−g∘∥∞2≤Cn−2β/(2β+d)})≥exp(−Cnd/(2​β+d)).\displaystyle\widetilde{\pi}_{\beta}\bigl(\bigl\{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\beta}:\|g-g^{\circ}\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq Cn^{-2\beta/(2\beta+d)}\bigr\}\bigr)\geq\exp\bigl(-Cn^{d/(2\beta+d)}\bigr).
(43)
Moreover, there exists C′>0C^{\prime}>0, depending only on β,d,c0,C0,S\beta,d,c_{0},C_{0},S and σ\sigma, such that
πβ​(Bβ​(Pg∘,ϵn,β))≥exp⁡(−C′​n​ϵn,β2),\displaystyle\pi_{\beta}\bigl(B_{\beta}(P_{g^{\circ}},\epsilon_{n,\beta})\bigr)\geq\exp\bigl(-C^{\prime}n\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}\bigr),
(44)
where ϵn,β≔C′n−β/(2β+d)\epsilon_{n,\beta}\coloneqq C^{\prime}n^{-\beta/(2\beta+d)} and Bβ​(Pg∘,ϵn,β)B_{\beta}(P_{g^{\circ}},\epsilon_{n,\beta}) is defined by (37).
Proof.
Throughout the proof, C1,C2,…C_{1},C_{2},\ldots will denote positive quantities that may depend only on β,d,c0,C0\beta,d,c_{0},C_{0} and SS, while C1′,C2′,…C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime},\ldots may in addition depend on σ\sigma. Let g~β\widetilde{g}_{\beta} be given by (8) so that g~β∼π~β\widetilde{g}_{\beta}\sim\widetilde{\pi}_{\beta}, and write ak≡ak(β)a_{k}\equiv a_{k}^{(\beta)} and bℓ,γ≡bℓ,γ(β)b_{\ell,\gamma}\equiv b_{\ell,\gamma}^{(\beta)} where the random variables (ak(β))(a_{k}^{(\beta)}) and (bℓ,γ(β))(b_{\ell,\gamma}^{(\beta)}) satisfy the conditions below (8). By (32) and (33), writing L≔⌈log2⁡n2​β+d⌉L\coloneqq\bigl\lceil\frac{\log_{2}n}{2\beta+d}\bigr\rceil (and assuming for now that L≥ℓ0L\geq\ell_{0}), we have
‖g~β−g∘‖∞\displaystyle\|\widetilde{g}_{\beta}-g^{\circ}\|_{\infty}
≤C1​maxk∈K​|ak−ak∘|+C1​∑ℓ=ℓ0L2−ℓ​β​maxγ∈Γℓ​|bℓ,γ−bℓ,γ∘|+2​C0​C1​∑ℓ=L+1∞2−ℓ​β\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\max_{k\in K}|a_{k}-a^{\circ}_{k}|+C_{1}\sum_{\ell=\ell_{0}}^{L}2^{-\ell\beta}\max_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{\ell}}|b_{\ell,\gamma}-b_{\ell,\gamma}^{\circ}|+2C_{0}C_{1}\sum_{\ell=L+1}^{\infty}2^{-\ell\beta}
≤C1maxk∈K|ak−a∘k|+C1∑ℓ=ℓ0L2−ℓ​βmaxγ∈Γℓ|bℓ,γ−bℓ,γ∘|+C3n−β/(2β+d).\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\max_{k\in K}|a_{k}-a^{\circ}_{k}|+C_{1}\sum_{\ell=\ell_{0}}^{L}2^{-\ell\beta}\max_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{\ell}}|b_{\ell,\gamma}-b_{\ell,\gamma}^{\circ}|+C_{3}n^{-\beta/(2\beta+d)}.
(45)
Define the events
ℰ1\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}
≔⋂k∈K{|ak−ak∘|≤2⋅n−β/(2β+d)},\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigcap_{k\in K}\Bigl\{|a_{k}-a^{\circ}_{k}|\leq 2\cdot n^{-\beta/(2\beta+d)}\Bigr\},
ℰ2\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}
≔⋂ℓ=ℓ0L⋂γ∈Γℓ{|bℓ,γ−bℓ,γ∘|≤2⋅2−2​(L−ℓ)​β}.\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigcap_{\ell=\ell_{0}}^{L}\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{\ell}}\Bigl\{|b_{\ell,\gamma}-b_{\ell,\gamma}^{\circ}|\leq 2\cdot 2^{-2(L-\ell)\beta}\Bigr\}.
Then, on the event ℰ1∩ℰ2\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}, we have by (45) that
‖g~β−g∘‖∞\displaystyle\|\widetilde{g}_{\beta}-g^{\circ}\|_{\infty}
≤2C1n−β/(2β+d)+2C12−L​β∑ℓ=ℓ0L2−(L−ℓ)​β+C3n−β/(2β+d)≤C4n−β/(2β+d).\displaystyle\leq 2C_{1}n^{-\beta/(2\beta+d)}+2C_{1}2^{-L\beta}\sum_{\ell=\ell_{0}}^{L}2^{-(L-\ell)\beta}+C_{3}n^{-\beta/(2\beta+d)}\leq C_{4}n^{-\beta/(2\beta+d)}.
Therefore,
ℙ(∥g~β−g∘∥∞≤C4n−β/(2β+d))\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\widetilde{g}_{\beta}-g^{\circ}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{4}n^{-\beta/(2\beta+d)}\bigr)
≥ℙ⁡(ℰ1∩ℰ2)=ℙ⁡(ℰ1)​ℙ​(ℰ2).\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2})=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1})\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{2}).
(46)
Now, by (31), we have
ℙ(ℰ1)≥(c0n−β/(2β+d))2ℓ0​d=exp(−C5logn).\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1})\geq\bigl(c_{0}n^{-\beta/(2\beta+d)}\bigr)^{2^{\ell_{0}d}}=\exp(-C_{5}\log n).
(47)
Similarly,
ℙ⁡(ℰ2)>∏ℓ=ℓ0L(c0​2−2​(L−ℓ)​β)2(ℓ+1)​d\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{2})>\prod_{\ell=\ell_{0}}^{L}\bigl(c_{0}2^{-2(L-\ell)\beta}\bigr)^{2^{(\ell+1)d}}
=exp⁡((log⁡c0)​∑ℓ=ℓ0L2(ℓ+1)​d−(2d+1​log⁡2)​β​∑ℓ=ℓ0L(L−ℓ)​2ℓ​d)\displaystyle=\exp\biggl((\log c_{0})\sum_{\ell=\ell_{0}}^{L}2^{(\ell+1)d}-(2^{d+1}\log 2)\beta\sum_{\ell=\ell_{0}}^{L}(L-\ell)2^{\ell d}\biggr)
≥exp⁡(−C6​nd/(2​β+d)),\displaystyle\geq\exp(-C_{6}n^{d/(2\beta+d)}),
(48)
where the final inequality follows since if we set A≔∑ℓ=ℓ0L(L−ℓ)​2ℓ​dA\coloneqq\sum_{\ell=\ell_{0}}^{L}(L-\ell)2^{\ell d}, then 2d​A−A≤∑ℓ=0L2ℓ​d≤2(L+1)​d/(2d−1)2^{d}A-A\leq\sum_{\ell=0}^{L}2^{\ell d}\leq 2^{(L+1)d}/(2^{d}-1), so A≤2(L+1)​d/(2d−1)2A\leq 2^{(L+1)d}/(2^{d}-1)^{2}.
The conclusion (43) for nn large enough that L≥ℓ0L\geq\ell_{0} follows from (46), (47) and (48). By increasing C>0C>0, depending only on β,d,c0,C0\beta,d,c_{0},C_{0} and SS, we can then ensure that (43) holds for all n∈ℕn\in\mathbb{N}.
For (44), note that by Lemma 24(a) and (b), we have that if g∈𝒢βg\in\mathcal{G}_{\beta} satisfies ∥g−g∘∥∞2≤Cn−2β/(2β+d)\|g-g^{\circ}\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq Cn^{-2\beta/(2\beta+d)}, then

Writing ϵn,β≔C1′​C⋅n−β/(2β+d)\epsilon_{n,\beta}\coloneqq\sqrt{C_{1}^{\prime}C}\cdot n^{-\beta/(2\beta+d)}, we deduce from (43) that
πβ​(Bβ​(Pg∘,ϵn,β))≥exp⁡(−C​nd/(2​β+d))≥exp⁡(−C2′​n​ϵn,β2).\displaystyle\pi_{\beta}\bigl(B_{\beta}(P_{g^{\circ}},\epsilon_{n,\beta})\bigr)\geq\exp\bigl(-Cn^{d/(2\beta+d)}\bigr)\geq\exp(-C_{2}^{\prime}n\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}).
Thus (44) holds with C′≔C1′​C∨C2′C^{\prime}\coloneqq\sqrt{C_{1}^{\prime}C}\vee C_{2}^{\prime}.
∎
Lemma 19 below provides an upper bound on the prior mass.
Lemma 19.

π~α({g∈𝒢α:∥g−g∘∥L2​(PX)2≤c04m2n−2α/(2α+d)})≤exp(−cm−d/αnd/(2​α+d))\displaystyle\widetilde{\pi}_{\alpha}\Bigl(\Bigl\{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}:\|g-g^{\circ}\|_{L^{2}(P_{X})}^{2}\leq\frac{c_{0}}{4}m^{2}n^{-2\alpha/(2\alpha+d)}\Bigr\}\Bigr)\leq\exp\bigl(-cm^{-d/\alpha}n^{d/(2\alpha+d)}\bigr)
(49)
for n≥n0n\geq n_{0}. Moreover, there exists c′>0c^{\prime}>0, depending only on α,β,d,c0,C0,S,τ\alpha,\beta,d,c_{0},C_{0},S,\tau and σ\sigma, such that for n≥n0n\geq n_{0},
πα(Bα′(Pg∘,c′mn−α/(2α+d)))≤exp(−cm−d/αnd/(2​α+d))\displaystyle\pi_{\alpha}\bigl(B^{\prime}_{\alpha}(P_{g^{\circ}},c^{\prime}mn^{-\alpha/(2\alpha+d)})\bigr)\leq\exp\bigl(-cm^{-d/\alpha}n^{d/(2\alpha+d)}\bigr)
(50)
where Bα′(Pg∘,c′mn−α/(2α+d))B^{\prime}_{\alpha}(P_{g^{\circ}},c^{\prime}mn^{-\alpha/(2\alpha+d)}) is defined as in (38).
Proof.

where |ak′|≤1|a_{k}^{\prime}|\leq 1 and |bℓ,γ′|≤1|b_{\ell,\gamma}^{\prime}|\leq 1. Thus,
π~α({g∈𝒢α:∥g\displaystyle\widetilde{\pi}_{\alpha}\Bigl(\Bigl\{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}:\|g

(51)
Let g~α∼π~α\widetilde{g}_{\alpha}\sim\widetilde{\pi}_{\alpha}, and write ak≡ak(α)a_{k}\equiv a_{k}^{(\alpha)} and bℓ,γ≡bℓ,γ(α)b_{\ell,\gamma}\equiv b_{\ell,\gamma}^{(\alpha)} with representation (8), where the random variables (ak(α))(a_{k}^{(\alpha)}) and (bℓ,γ(α))(b_{\ell,\gamma}^{(\alpha)}) satisfy the conditions below (8). Further, let C1≔⌈log2⁡(2/τ)⌉C_{1}\coloneqq\lceil\log_{2}(2/\tau)\rceil. Then, by the assumption on PXP_{X}, there exists C2∈[0:2C1−1]C_{2}\in[0:2^{C_{1}}-1] such that [C22C1,C2+12C1]d⊆A\bigl[\frac{C_{2}}{2^{C_{1}}},\frac{C_{2}+1}{2^{C_{1}}}\bigr]^{d}\subseteq A. Assume henceforth that n≥n0n\geq n_{0}. Let ΓL′≔{γ∈ΓL:supp⁡(ΨL,γ)⊆[C22C1,C2+12C1]d}\Gamma_{L}^{\prime}\coloneqq\bigl\{\gamma\in\Gamma_{L}:\mathrm{supp}(\Psi_{L,\gamma})\subseteq\bigl[\frac{C_{2}}{2^{C_{1}}},\frac{C_{2}+1}{2^{C_{1}}}\bigr]^{d}\bigr\}, bL≔(bL,γ)γ∈ΓL′b_{L}\coloneqq(b_{L,\gamma})_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{L}^{\prime}} and bL′≔(bL,γ′)γ∈ΓL′b_{L}^{\prime}\coloneqq(b_{L,\gamma}^{\prime})_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{L}^{\prime}}. ≥|ΓL′|​C02​2−L⁡(2​α+d)⋅c03≥c0​C02​2−2​α​L−C1​d3≥2​m2​ϵ2.\displaystyle\geq|\Gamma_{L}^{\prime}|C_{0}^{2}2^{-L(2\alpha+d)}\cdot\frac{c_{0}}{3}\geq\frac{c_{0}C_{0}^{2}2^{-2\alpha L-C_{1}d}}{3}\geq 2m^{2}\epsilon^{2}.
Therefore, by the assumption on PXP_{X} and Lemma 25, we have
ℙ⁡(‖g~α−g′‖L2​(PX)2≤c0CLOSE\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\widetilde{g}_{\alpha}-g^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(P_{X})}^{2}\leq c_{0}
OPENm2​ϵ2)≤ℙ⁡(c0​‖g~α−g′‖L2​([C2​2−C1,(C2+1)​2−C1]d)2≤c0​m2​ϵ2)\displaystyle m^{2}\epsilon^{2}\bigr)\leq\mathbb{P}\bigl(c_{0}\|\widetilde{g}_{\alpha}-g^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}([C_{2}2^{-C_{1}},(C_{2}+1)2^{-C_{1}}]^{d})}^{2}\leq c_{0}m^{2}\epsilon^{2}\bigr)
≤ℙ{∥bL−bL′∥22−𝔼(∥bL−bL′∥22)≤m2​ϵ2C02​2−L⁡(2​α+d)−𝔼(∥bL−bL′∥22)}\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\biggl\{\|b_{L}-b_{L}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\bigl(\|b_{L}-b_{L}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr)\leq\frac{m^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{C_{0}^{2}2^{-L(2\alpha+d)}}-\mathbb{E}\bigl(\|b_{L}-b_{L}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr)\biggr\}
≤ℙ{∥bL−bL′∥22−𝔼(∥bL−bL′∥22)≤−m2​ϵ2C02​2−L⁡(2​α+d)}.\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\biggl\{\|b_{L}-b_{L}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\bigl(\|b_{L}-b_{L}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr)\leq-\frac{m^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{C_{0}^{2}2^{-L(2\alpha+d)}}\biggr\}.
(52)
Since (bL,γ−bL,γ′)2∈[0,4](b_{L,\gamma}-b_{L,\gamma}^{\prime})^{2}\in[0,4] for γ∈ΓL′\gamma\in\Gamma_{L}^{\prime}, we have by Hoeffding’s inequality and (31) that
ℙ{∥bL\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\biggl\{\|b_{L}

where c>0c>0 depends only on α,d,c0,C0\alpha,d,c_{0},C_{0} and τ\tau. Combining (51), (52) and (53) yields (49).
For the second claim, note that by (32) and (33),

(54)
and similarly, ‖g~α‖∞≤C2′\|\widetilde{g}_{\alpha}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{2}^{\prime} almost surely. Thus, if dH(Pg∘,Pg)≤c′mn−α/(2α+d)d_{\mathrm{H}}(P_{g^{\circ}},P_{g})\leq c^{\prime}mn^{-\alpha/(2\alpha+d)} for some c′>0c^{\prime}>0, then by Lemma 24(d),

Therefore, taking c′≔c0/(4​C3′)c^{\prime}\coloneqq\sqrt{c_{0}/(4C_{3}^{\prime})} and applying (49) proves (50).

We are now in a position to prove Proposition 4.
Proof of Proposition 4.
Throughout the proof, C1,C2,…C_{1},C_{2},\ldots will denote positive quantities that do not depend on nn. By an argument similar to (54), there exists C1>0C_{1}>0 such that ‖g∘‖∞≤C1\|g^{\circ}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{1} and ‖g‖∞≤C1\|g\|_{\infty}\leq C_{1} for all g∈𝒢g\in\mathcal{G}.
We now verify the assumptions of Corollary 17 with H=1H=1. For each α∈𝒜\alpha\in\mathcal{A}, let C′>0C^{\prime}>0 (which does not depend on nn) be the quantity in Lemma 18 such that (44) holds, and let ϵn,α≔C′n−α/(2α+d)\epsilon_{n,\alpha}\coloneqq C^{\prime}n^{-\alpha/(2\alpha+d)} for α∈𝒜\alpha\in\mathcal{A}. Then by Lemma 18, condition (C2) holds with F=C′F=C^{\prime}. By Lemma 24(d), (9) and Giné and Nickl (2021, Eq. (4.184)), for each α∈𝒜\alpha\in\mathcal{A}, we have
supϵ≥ϵn,αlog⁡𝒩⁡(ϵ/3,Bα′​(P0,2​ϵ),dH)\displaystyle\sup_{\epsilon\geq\epsilon_{n,\alpha}}\log\mathcal{N}\bigl(\epsilon/3,\,B^{\prime}_{\alpha}(P_{0},2\epsilon),\,d_{\mathrm{H}}\bigr)
≤log⁡𝒩⁡(ϵn,α/3,𝒫α,dH)\displaystyle\leq\log\mathcal{N}\bigl(\epsilon_{n,\alpha}/3,\,\mathcal{P}_{\alpha},\,d_{\mathrm{H}}\bigr)
≤log𝒩(2σϵn,α/3,𝒢α,∥⋅∥∞)\displaystyle\leq\log\mathcal{N}\bigl(2\sigma\epsilon_{n,\alpha}/3,\,\mathcal{G}_{\alpha},\,\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\bigr)
≤log𝒩(2σϵn,α/3,B∞,∞α([0,1]d,2C0),∥⋅∥∞)\displaystyle\leq\log\mathcal{N}\bigl(2\sigma\epsilon_{n,\alpha}/3,\,B^{\alpha}_{\infty,\infty}([0,1]^{d},2C_{0}),\,\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\bigr)
≤Eα​n​ϵn,α2,\displaystyle\leq E_{\alpha}n\epsilon_{n,\alpha}^{2},
for some Eα>0E_{\alpha}>0 not depending on nn; this verifies condition (C1). Moreover, since 𝒜\mathcal{A} is finite, condition (C3) is satisfied with μn,α=1\mu_{n,\alpha}=1 for all α∈𝒜\alpha\in\mathcal{A} when nn is sufficiently large. Similarly, condition (C5) is satisfied for some E>0E>0 not depending on nn. Finally, take M>0M>0 such that M2>243​(F+1)​(H​E+1)M^{2}>243(F+1)(HE+1). Letting c,c′>0c,c^{\prime}>0 be the quantities in Lemma 19 and applying Lemma 19 with m≔M​C′/c′m\coloneqq MC^{\prime}/c^{\prime}, we deduce that for nn sufficiently large,
∑α∈𝒜<βλαλβ⋅πα(Bα′(P0,Mϵn,α))≤∑α∈𝒜:α<βλαλβ⋅exp(−cm−d/αnd/(2​α+d))≤e−(F+3)​n​ϵn,β2,\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}_{<\beta}}\frac{\lambda_{\alpha}}{\lambda_{\beta}}\cdot\pi_{\alpha}\bigl(B^{\prime}_{\alpha}(P_{0},M\epsilon_{n,\alpha})\bigr)\leq\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}:\alpha<\beta}\frac{\lambda_{\alpha}}{\lambda_{\beta}}\cdot\exp\bigl(-cm^{-d/\alpha}n^{d/(2\alpha+d)}\bigr)\leq e^{-(F+3)n\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}},
which verifies condition (C4). Therefore, by Corollary 17, the claim follows for sufficiently large nn, since e−c3​n​ϵn,β2≤ϵn,β2e^{-c_{3}n\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2}}\leq\epsilon_{n,\beta}^{2} for nn large enough. Moreover, since gπg_{\pi} and g∘g^{\circ} are bounded, the conclusion now follows for all n∈ℕn\in\mathbb{N} by increasing C>0C>0 in the statement if necessary.
∎
F.2 Proof of Proposition 6
We work in the setting of Section 4.2. For (α,p)∈𝒜′(\alpha,p)\in\mathcal{A}^{\prime}, we define 𝒢α(p)\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}^{(p)} similarly to the definition of 𝒢α\mathcal{G}_{\alpha} in Section 4.1 but replacing the dimensionality dd by pp, i.e. we let 𝒢α(p)\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}^{(p)} be the set of all functions hα(p):[0,1]p→ℝh_{\alpha}^{(p)}:[0,1]^{p}\to\mathbb{R} such that hh has wavelet decomposition
hα(p)=∑k∈KC02−ℓ0r/2akΦk(p)+∑ℓ=ℓ0∞∑γ∈ΓℓC02−ℓ⁡(β+r/2)bℓ,γΨℓ,γ(p),\displaystyle h_{\alpha}^{(p)}=\sum_{k\in K}C_{0}2^{-\ell_{0}r/2}a_{k}\Phi_{k}^{(p)}+\sum_{\ell=\ell_{0}}^{\infty}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{\ell}}C_{0}2^{-\ell(\beta+r/2)}b_{\ell,\gamma}\Psi_{\ell,\gamma}^{(p)},
where (Φk(p))(\Phi_{k}^{(p)}) and (Ψℓ,γ(p))(\Psi_{\ell,\gamma}^{(p)}) is the tensor product CDV wavelet basis for L2​([0,1]p)L^{2}([0,1]^{p}), |ak|≤1|a_{k}|\leq 1 and |bℓ,γ|≤1|b_{\ell,\gamma}|\leq 1. Thus, similarly to (9),
B∞,∞α​([0,1]p,C0)⊆𝒢α(p)⊆B∞,∞α​([0,1]p,2​C0).\displaystyle B_{\infty,\infty}^{\alpha}([0,1]^{p},C_{0})\subseteq\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}^{(p)}\subseteq B_{\infty,\infty}^{\alpha}([0,1]^{p},2C_{0}).
The proof of Proposition 6 relies on three preliminary lemmas that we now formulate. We may write
g∘​(x)=h∘​((U∘)⊤​x+𝟏r2),\displaystyle g^{\circ}(x)=h^{\circ}\biggl(\frac{(U^{\circ})^{\top}x+\bm{1}_{r}}{2}\biggr),
(55)
where U∘∈Vr​(ℝd)U^{\circ}\in V_{r}(\mathbb{R}^{d}) and h∘∈𝒢β(r)h^{\circ}\in\mathcal{G}_{\beta}^{(r)} has wavelet decomposition
h∘=∑k∈KC02−ℓ0r/2ak∘Φk(r)+∑ℓ=ℓ0∞∑γ∈ΓℓC02−ℓ⁡(β+r/2)bℓ,γ∘Ψℓ,γ(r),\displaystyle h^{\circ}=\sum_{k\in K}C_{0}2^{-\ell_{0}r/2}a_{k}^{\circ}\Phi_{k}^{(r)}+\sum_{\ell=\ell_{0}}^{\infty}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{\ell}}C_{0}2^{-\ell(\beta+r/2)}b_{\ell,\gamma}^{\circ}\Psi_{\ell,\gamma}^{(r)},
(56)
where |ak∘|≤1|a^{\circ}_{k}|\leq 1 and |bℓ,γ∘|≤1|b^{\circ}_{\ell,\gamma}|\leq 1.
Lemma 20.
Let n∈ℕn\in\mathbb{N} and assume that (55) and (56) hold. Then there exists C>0C>0, depending only on β,d,r,c0,C0\beta,d,r,c_{0},C_{0} and SS, such that
π~β,r({g∈𝒢β,r:∥g−g∘∥∞2≤Cn−2β/(2β+r)})≥exp(−Cnr/(2​β+r)).\displaystyle\widetilde{\pi}_{\beta,r}\bigl(\bigl\{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\beta,r}:\|g-g^{\circ}\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq Cn^{-2\beta/(2\beta+r)}\bigr\}\bigr)\geq\exp\bigl(-Cn^{r/(2\beta+r)}\bigr).
(57)
Moreover, there exists C′>0C^{\prime}>0, depending only on β,d,r,c0,C0,S\beta,d,r,c_{0},C_{0},S and σ\sigma, such that if ϵn,β,r≔C′n−β/(2β+r)\epsilon_{n,\beta,r}\coloneqq C^{\prime}n^{-\beta/(2\beta+r)}, then
πβ,r​(Bβ,r​(Pg∘,ϵn,β,r))≥exp⁡(−C′​n​ϵn,β,r2),\displaystyle\pi_{\beta,r}\bigl(B_{\beta,r}(P_{g^{\circ}},\epsilon_{n,\beta,r})\bigr)\geq\exp\bigl(-C^{\prime}n\epsilon_{n,\beta,r}^{2}\bigr),
(58)
where Bβ,r(Pg∘,ϵn,β,r)≔{P∈𝒫β,r:KL(Pg∘,P)≤ϵn,β,r2,V2(Pg∘,P)≤ϵn,β,r2}B_{\beta,r}(P_{g^{\circ}},\epsilon_{n,\beta,r})\coloneqq\bigl\{P\in\mathcal{P}_{\beta,r}:\mathrm{KL}(P_{g^{\circ}},P)\leq\epsilon_{n,\beta,r}^{2},\,\mathrm{V}_{2}(P_{g^{\circ}},P)\leq\epsilon_{n,\beta,r}^{2}\bigr\}, similarly to (37).
Proof.
Throughout the proof, C1,C2,…C_{1},C_{2},\ldots will denote positive quantities that may depend only on β,d,r,c0,C0\beta,d,r,c_{0},C_{0} and SS, while C1′,C2′,…C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime},\ldots may in addition depend on σ\sigma.
Let g~β,r​(x)=g~β(r)​((U(r))⊤​x+𝟏r2)\widetilde{g}_{\beta,r}(x)=\widetilde{g}_{\beta}^{(r)}\bigl(\frac{(U^{(r)})^{\top}x+\bm{1}_{r}}{2}\bigr) be defined as in (11) so that g~β,r∼π~β,r\widetilde{g}_{\beta,r}\sim\widetilde{\pi}_{\beta,r}. Observe that B∞,∞β​([0,1]r,2​C0)⊆B∞,∞β∧1​([0,1]r,2​C0)B_{\infty,\infty}^{\beta}([0,1]^{r},2C_{0})\subseteq B_{\infty,\infty}^{\beta\wedge 1}([0,1]^{r},2C_{0}) (e.g. Giné and Nickl, 2021, Proposition 4.3.10(ii)). When β<1\beta<1, the space B∞,∞β∧1B_{\infty,\infty}^{\beta\wedge 1} is equal to the Hölder space HβH^{\beta} with equivalent norms (e.g. Giné and Nickl, 2021, Proposition 4.3.23), so for all x,y∈[0,1]rx,y\in[0,1]^{r} and g∈B∞,∞β∧1​([0,1]r,2​C0)g\in B_{\infty,\infty}^{\beta\wedge 1}([0,1]^{r},2C_{0}), we have |g⁡(x)−g⁡(y)|≤C1​‖x−y‖2β|g(x)-g(y)|\leq C_{1}\|x-y\|_{2}^{\beta}; when β≥1\beta\geq 1, B∞,∞β∧1B_{\infty,\infty}^{\beta\wedge 1} is equal to the Zygmund space of order 11 with equivalent norms (Triebel, 1983, p. 113), so by Anderson and Pitt (1989, Proposition 2.4) (see also Zygmund, 2002, Chapter II, Theorem 3.4), for all x,y∈[0,1]rx,y\in[0,1]^{r} and g∈B∞,∞β∧1​([0,1]r,2​C0)g\in B_{\infty,\infty}^{\beta\wedge 1}([0,1]^{r},2C_{0}), we have |g⁡(x)−g⁡(y)|≤C2​‖x−y‖2​{log⁡(1/‖x−y‖2)∨1}|g(x)-g(y)|\leq C_{2}\|x-y\|_{2}\bigl\{\log(1/\|x-y\|_{2})\vee 1\bigr\}. Therefore, for all x,y∈[0,1]rx,y\in[0,1]^{r} and g∈B∞,∞β​([0,1]r,2​C0)g\in B_{\infty,\infty}^{\beta}([0,1]^{r},2C_{0}),
|g⁡(x)−g⁡(y)|≤C3​‖x−y‖2β∧1​{log⁡(1/‖x−y‖2)∨1}.\displaystyle|g(x)-g(y)|\leq C_{3}\|x-y\|_{2}^{\beta\wedge 1}\bigl\{\log(1/\|x-y\|_{2})\vee 1\bigr\}.
(59)
On the event E≔{∥U(r)−U∘∥op≤(n−β/(2β+r)/log(en))1/(β∧1)}E\coloneqq\bigl\{\|U^{(r)}-U^{\circ}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\bigl(n^{-\beta/(2\beta+r)}/\log(en)\bigr)^{1/(\beta\wedge 1)}\bigr\}, we have for all x∈𝔹dx\in\mathbb{B}^{d} that
|g~β,r​(x)−g∘​(x)|\displaystyle|\widetilde{g}_{\beta,r}(x)-g^{\circ}(x)|
≤|g~β(r)​((U(r))⊤​x+𝟏r2)−g~β(r)​((U∘)⊤​x+𝟏r2)|+|g~β(r)​((U∘)⊤​x+𝟏r2)−h∘​((U∘)⊤​x+𝟏r2)|\displaystyle\leq\biggl|\widetilde{g}_{\beta}^{(r)}\biggl(\frac{(U^{(r)})^{\top}x+\bm{1}_{r}}{2}\biggr)-\widetilde{g}_{\beta}^{(r)}\biggl(\frac{(U^{\circ})^{\top}x+\bm{1}_{r}}{2}\biggr)\biggr|+\biggl|\widetilde{g}_{\beta}^{(r)}\biggl(\frac{(U^{\circ})^{\top}x+\bm{1}_{r}}{2}\biggr)-h^{\circ}\biggl(\frac{(U^{\circ})^{\top}x+\bm{1}_{r}}{2}\biggr)\biggr|
≤C4n−β/{(2β+r)}+∥g~β(r)−h∘∥∞,\displaystyle\leq C_{4}n^{-\beta/\{(2\beta+r)\}}+\|\widetilde{g}_{\beta}^{(r)}-h^{\circ}\|_{\infty},
(60)
where the second inequality follows from (59), since g~β(r)∈𝒢β(r)⊆B∞,∞β​([0,1]r,2​C0)\widetilde{g}_{\beta}^{(r)}\in\mathcal{G}_{\beta}^{(r)}\subseteq B_{\infty,\infty}^{\beta}([0,1]^{r},2C_{0}) and ∥(U(r))⊤​x+𝟏r2−(U∘)⊤​x+𝟏r2∥2≤12(n−β/(2β+r)/log(en))1/(β∧1)\bigl\|\frac{(U^{(r)})^{\top}x+\bm{1}_{r}}{2}-\frac{(U^{\circ})^{\top}x+\bm{1}_{r}}{2}\bigr\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\bigl(n^{-\beta/(2\beta+r)}/\log(en)\bigr)^{1/(\beta\wedge 1)} on EE. Now by Lemma 26, we have
ℙ⁡(E)≥C5​(n−β/(2β+r)log⁡(e​n)∧12)d​r/(β∧1)≥exp⁡(−C6​nr/(2​β+r)),\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(E)\geq C_{5}\biggl(\frac{n^{-\beta/(2\beta+r)}}{\log(en)}\wedge\frac{1}{2}\biggr)^{dr/(\beta\wedge 1)}\geq\exp(-C_{6}n^{r/(2\beta+r)}),
and by (43) (with dd replaced by rr therein), we have
ℙ(∥g~β(r)−h∘∥∞≤C7n−β/(2β+r))≥exp(−C7nr/(2​β+r)).\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\widetilde{g}_{\beta}^{(r)}-h^{\circ}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{7}n^{-\beta/(2\beta+r)}\bigr)\geq\exp(-C_{7}n^{r/(2\beta+r)}).
Hence,
ℙ⁡(‖g~β,r−g∘‖∞2CLOSE\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\widetilde{g}_{\beta,r}-g^{\circ}\|_{\infty}^{2}
≤(C4+C7)2n−2β/(2β+r))\displaystyle\leq(C_{4}+C_{7})^{2}n^{-2\beta/(2\beta+r)}\bigr)
≥ℙ(∥g~β,r−g∘∥∞≤(C4+C7)n−β/(2β+r)|E)ℙ(E)\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\widetilde{g}_{\beta,r}-g^{\circ}\|_{\infty}\leq(C_{4}+C_{7})n^{-\beta/(2\beta+r)}\,\big|\,E\bigr)\mathbb{P}(E)
≥ℙ(∥g~β(r)−h∘∥∞≤C7n−β/(2β+r))ℙ(E)≥exp{−(C6+C7)nr/(2​β+r)},\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\widetilde{g}_{\beta}^{(r)}-h^{\circ}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{7}n^{-\beta/(2\beta+r)}\bigr)\mathbb{P}(E)\geq\exp\bigl\{-(C_{6}+C_{7})n^{r/(2\beta+r)}\bigr\},
where the second inequality follows from (60) and the fact that g~β(r)\widetilde{g}_{\beta}^{(r)} and U(r)U^{(r)} are independent. This proves (57) with C≔(C4+C7)2∨(C6+C7)C\coloneqq(C_{4}+C_{7})^{2}\vee(C_{6}+C_{7}).
The proof of (58) follows the same argument as the proof of (44). By Lemma 24(a) and (b), if g∈𝒢β,rg\in\mathcal{G}_{\beta,r} satisfies ∥g−g∘∥∞2≤Cn−2β/(2β+r)\|g-g^{\circ}\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq Cn^{-2\beta/(2\beta+r)}, then
KL(Pg∘,Pg)≤C1′Cn−2β/(2β+r)andV2(Pg∘,Pg)≤C2′Cn−2β/(2β+r).\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(P_{g^{\circ}},P_{g})\leq C_{1}^{\prime}Cn^{-2\beta/(2\beta+r)}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{V}_{2}(P_{g^{\circ}},P_{g})\leq C_{2}^{\prime}Cn^{-2\beta/(2\beta+r)}.
Writing C′≔C1/3∨(C1′∨C2′)​CC^{\prime}\coloneqq C^{1/3}\vee\sqrt{(C_{1}^{\prime}\vee C_{2}^{\prime})C} and ϵn,β,r≔C′n−β/(2β+r)\epsilon_{n,\beta,r}\coloneqq C^{\prime}n^{-\beta/(2\beta+r)}, we deduce from (57) that
πβ,r​(Bβ,r​(Pg∘,ϵn,β,r))≥exp⁡(−C​nr/(2​β+r))≥exp⁡(−C′​n​ϵn,β,r2).\displaystyle\pi_{\beta,r}\bigl(B_{\beta,r}(P_{g^{\circ}},\epsilon_{n,\beta,r})\bigr)\geq\exp\bigl(-Cn^{r/(2\beta+r)}\bigr)\geq\exp(-C^{\prime}n\epsilon_{n,\beta,r}^{2}).
This proves the claim.
∎
Lemma 21.

π~α,p({g∈𝒢α,p:∥g−g∘∥L2​(PX)2≤c0′m2n−2α/(2α+p)})≤exp(−cm−p/αnp/(2​α+p))\displaystyle\widetilde{\pi}_{\alpha,p}\Bigl(\Bigl\{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,p}:\|g-g^{\circ}\|_{L^{2}(P_{X})}^{2}\leq c_{0}^{\prime}m^{2}n^{-2\alpha/(2\alpha+p)}\Bigr\}\Bigr)\leq\exp\bigl(-cm^{-p/\alpha}n^{p/(2\alpha+p)}\bigr)
(61)
for n≥n0n\geq n_{0}. Moreover, there exists c′>0c^{\prime}>0, depending only on α,β,d,p,c0,C0,S,τ\alpha,\beta,d,p,c_{0},C_{0},S,\tau and σ\sigma, such that for n≥n0n\geq n_{0},
πα,p(Bα,p′(Pg∘,c′mn−α/(2α+p)))≤exp(−cm−p/αnp/(2​α+p))\displaystyle\pi_{\alpha,p}\bigl(B^{\prime}_{\alpha,p}(P_{g^{\circ}},c^{\prime}mn^{-\alpha/(2\alpha+p)})\bigr)\leq\exp\bigl(-cm^{-p/\alpha}n^{p/(2\alpha+p)}\bigr)
(62)
where Bα,p′(Pg∘,c′mn−α/(2α+p))≔{P∈𝒫α,p:dH(Pg∘,P)≤c′mn−α/(2α+p)}B^{\prime}_{\alpha,p}(P_{g^{\circ}},c^{\prime}mn^{-\alpha/(2\alpha+p)})\coloneqq\bigl\{P\in\mathcal{P}_{\alpha,p}:d_{\mathrm{H}}(P_{g^{\circ}},P)\leq c^{\prime}mn^{-\alpha/(2\alpha+p)}\bigr\} is defined as in (38).
Proof.
Throughout the proof, C1,C2,…C_{1},C_{2},\ldots will denote positive quantities that may depend only on α,d,p,c0,C0\alpha,d,p,c_{0},C_{0} and τ\tau. Let c0′≡c0′​(c0,τ,d,p)c_{0}^{\prime}\equiv c_{0}^{\prime}(c_{0},\tau,d,p) be the quantity in Lemma 27.

Then
π~α,p\displaystyle\widetilde{\pi}_{\alpha,p}
({g∈𝒢α,p:∥g−g∘∥L2​(PX)2≤c0′m2n−2α/(2α+p)})\displaystyle\Bigl(\Bigl\{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,p}:\|g-g^{\circ}\|_{L^{2}(P_{X})}^{2}\leq c_{0}^{\prime}m^{2}n^{-2\alpha/(2\alpha+p)}\Bigr\}\Bigr)
=ℙ{∥g~α(p)((U(p))⊤​x+𝟏p2)−h∘((U∘)⊤​x+𝟏p2)∥L2​(PX)2≤c0′m2n−2α/(2α+p)}\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\biggl\{\biggl\|\widetilde{g}_{\alpha}^{(p)}\biggl(\frac{(U^{(p)})^{\top}x+\bm{1}_{p}}{2}\biggr)-h^{\circ}\biggl(\frac{(U^{\circ})^{\top}x+\bm{1}_{p}}{2}\biggr)\biggr\|_{L^{2}(P_{X})}^{2}\leq c_{0}^{\prime}m^{2}n^{-2\alpha/(2\alpha+p)}\biggr\}
=𝔼[ℙ{∥g~α(p)((U(p))⊤​x+𝟏p2)−h∘((U∘)⊤​x+𝟏p2)∥L2​(PX)2≤c0′m2n−2α/(2α+p)|U(p)}].\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathbb{P}\biggl\{\biggl\|\widetilde{g}_{\alpha}^{(p)}\biggl(\frac{(U^{(p)})^{\top}x+\bm{1}_{p}}{2}\biggr)-h^{\circ}\biggl(\frac{(U^{\circ})^{\top}x+\bm{1}_{p}}{2}\biggr)\biggr\|_{L^{2}(P_{X})}^{2}\leq c_{0}^{\prime}m^{2}n^{-2\alpha/(2\alpha+p)}\,\bigg|\,U^{(p)}\biggr\}\biggr].
(63)

ℙ\displaystyle\mathbb{P}
{∥g~α(p)((U(p))⊤​x+12)−h∘((U∘)⊤​x+𝟏p2)∥L2​(PX)2≤c0′m2n−2α/(2α+p)|U(p)}\displaystyle\biggl\{\biggl\|\widetilde{g}_{\alpha}^{(p)}\biggl(\frac{(U^{(p)})^{\top}x+1}{2}\biggr)-h^{\circ}\biggl(\frac{(U^{\circ})^{\top}x+\bm{1}_{p}}{2}\biggr)\biggr\|_{L^{2}(P_{X})}^{2}\leq c_{0}^{\prime}m^{2}n^{-2\alpha/(2\alpha+p)}\,\bigg|\,U^{(p)}\biggr\}

(64)
where Q⁡(U(p))Q(U^{(p)}) denotes the conditional distribution of (U(p))⊤​X+𝟏p2\frac{(U^{(p)})^{\top}X+\bm{1}_{p}}{2} given U(p)U^{(p)}, with X∼PXX\sim P_{X}. for n≥n0n\geq n_{0}.
Combining this with (63) and (64) proves (61).
Finally, the proof of (62) follows a very similar argument to that in (50).
∎
Lemma 22.
Let (α,p)∈𝒜(\alpha,p)\in\mathcal{A}, C>0C>0, P0∈𝒫α,pP_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{\alpha,p} and ϵn,α,p≔Cn−α/(2α+p)\epsilon_{n,\alpha,p}\coloneqq Cn^{-\alpha/(2\alpha+p)}. Then there exists C′C^{\prime} depending only on α,p,d,σ,C\alpha,p,d,\sigma,C and C0C_{0} such that
supϵ≥ϵn,α,plog⁡𝒩⁡(ϵ/3,Bα,p′​(P0,2​ϵ),dH)≤C′​n​ϵn,α,p2.\displaystyle\sup_{\epsilon\geq\epsilon_{n,\alpha,p}}\log\mathcal{N}\bigl(\epsilon/3,B_{\alpha,p}^{\prime}(P_{0},2\epsilon),d_{\mathrm{H}}\bigr)\leq C^{\prime}n\epsilon_{n,\alpha,p}^{2}.
Proof.
Throughout this proof, C1,C2,…C_{1},C_{2},\ldots will denote positive quantities depending only on α,p,d,σ,C\alpha,p,d,\sigma,C and C0C_{0}.
By Lemma 24(d),

≤log⁡𝒩⁡(ϵn,α,p/3,𝒫α,p,dH)\displaystyle\leq\log\mathcal{N}\bigl(\epsilon_{n,\alpha,p}/3,\,\mathcal{P}_{\alpha,p},\,d_{\mathrm{H}}\bigr)
≤log𝒩(2σϵn,α,p/3,𝒢α,p,∥⋅∥∞).\displaystyle\leq\log\mathcal{N}\bigl(2\sigma\epsilon_{n,\alpha,p}/3,\,\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,p},\,\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\bigr).
Recall that 𝒢α(p)⊆B∞,∞α​([0,1]p,2​C0)\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}^{(p)}\subseteq B_{\infty,\infty}^{\alpha}([0,1]^{p},2C_{0}) and 𝒢α,p=𝒢α(p)∘{x↦U⊤​x+𝟏p2:U∈Vp​(ℝd)}\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,p}=\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}^{(p)}\circ\bigl\{x\mapsto\frac{U^{\top}x+\bm{1}_{p}}{2}:U\in V_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\bigr\}. Now let 𝒩1\mathcal{N}_{1} be a (σ​ϵn,α,p/3)(\sigma\epsilon_{n,\alpha,p}/3)-cover of B∞,∞α​([0,1]p,2​C0)B_{\infty,\infty}^{\alpha}([0,1]^{p},2C_{0}) with respect to ∥⋅∥∞\|\cdot\|_{\infty} such that |𝒩1|≤exp⁡(C1​n​ϵn,α,p2)|\mathcal{N}_{1}|\leq\exp(C_{1}n\epsilon_{n,\alpha,p}^{2}) for some C1>0C_{1}>0, which exists by, e.g. Giné and Nickl (2021, Eq. (4.184)). Recall from (59) that for all x,y∈[0,1]px,y\in[0,1]^{p},
|g⁡(x)−g⁡(y)|≤C2​‖x−y‖2α∧1​{log⁡(1/‖x−y‖2)∨1}.\displaystyle|g(x)-g(y)|\leq C_{2}\|x-y\|_{2}^{\alpha\wedge 1}\bigl\{\log(1/\|x-y\|_{2})\vee 1\bigr\}.
(65)
The function z↦zα∧1​log⁡(1/z)z\mapsto z^{\alpha\wedge 1}\log(1/z) is increasing on (0,e−1/(α∧1)](0,e^{-1/(\alpha\wedge 1)}], so let δ≔1p​d(σ​ϵn,α,p3​C3​log⁡(e​n))1/(α∧1)∧1p​de−1/(α∧1)\delta\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{pd}}\bigl(\frac{\sigma\epsilon_{n,\alpha,p}}{3C_{3}\log(en)}\bigr)^{1/(\alpha\wedge 1)}\wedge\frac{1}{\sqrt{pd}}e^{-1/(\alpha\wedge 1)} for C3>0C_{3}>0 large enough such that
C2​(δ​p​d)α∧1​{log⁡(1δ​p​d)∨1}≤σ​ϵn,α,p3.\displaystyle C_{2}(\delta\sqrt{pd})^{\alpha\wedge 1}\biggl\{\log\biggl(\frac{1}{\delta\sqrt{pd}}\biggr)\vee 1\biggr\}\leq\frac{\sigma\epsilon_{n,\alpha,p}}{3}.
(66)
Let Δ≔{iδ:i∈ℤ,|iδ|≤1}\Delta\coloneqq\{i\delta:i\in\mathbb{Z},|i\delta|\leq 1\} and 𝒩2≔Δd×p⊆ℝd×p\mathcal{N}_{2}\coloneqq\Delta^{d\times p}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d\times p}. Then for any g∈𝒢α(p)⊆B∞,∞α​([0,1]p,2​C0)g\in\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}^{(p)}\subseteq B_{\infty,\infty}^{\alpha}([0,1]^{p},2C_{0}) and U∈Vp​(ℝd)U\in V_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}), there exist g′∈𝒩1g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{1} and U′∈𝒩2U^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{2} such that ‖g′−g‖∞≤σ​ϵn,α,p/3\|g^{\prime}-g\|_{\infty}\leq\sigma\epsilon_{n,\alpha,p}/3 and ‖U′−U‖op≤p​d​‖U′−U‖∞≤δ​p​d\|U^{\prime}-U\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{pd}\|U^{\prime}-U\|_{\infty}\leq\delta\sqrt{pd}. Thus, for all x∈𝔹dx\in\mathbb{B}^{d}, we have ‖U⊤​x−(U′)⊤​x‖2≤δ​p​d\|U^{\top}x-(U^{\prime})^{\top}x\|_{2}\leq\delta\sqrt{pd}, so by (65) and (66), we deduce that
|g(\displaystyle\biggl|g\biggl(
U⊤​x+𝟏p2)−g′((U′)⊤​x+𝟏p2)|\displaystyle\frac{U^{\top}x+\bm{1}_{p}}{2}\biggr)-g^{\prime}\biggl(\frac{(U^{\prime})^{\top}x+\bm{1}_{p}}{2}\biggr)\biggr|
≤|g⁡(U⊤​x+𝟏p2)−g⁡((U′)⊤​x+𝟏p2)|+|g⁡((U′)⊤​x+𝟏p2)−g′​((U′)⊤​x+𝟏p2)|\displaystyle\leq\biggl|g\biggl(\frac{U^{\top}x+\bm{1}_{p}}{2}\biggr)-g\biggl(\frac{(U^{\prime})^{\top}x+\bm{1}_{p}}{2}\biggr)\biggr|+\biggl|g\biggl(\frac{(U^{\prime})^{\top}x+\bm{1}_{p}}{2}\biggr)-g^{\prime}\biggl(\frac{(U^{\prime})^{\top}x+\bm{1}_{p}}{2}\biggr)\biggr|
≤2​σ​ϵn,α,p3.\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\sigma\epsilon_{n,\alpha,p}}{3}.
Therefore, 𝒩1∘𝒩2\mathcal{N}_{1}\circ\mathcal{N}_{2} is a (2​σ​ϵn,α,p/3)(2\sigma\epsilon_{n,\alpha,p}/3)-cover of 𝒢α,p\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,p} with respect to ∥⋅∥∞\|\cdot\|_{\infty}. Hence, we have
log𝒩(2σϵn,α,p/3,𝒢α,p,∥⋅∥∞)≤log|𝒩1|+log|𝒩2|≤C′nϵn,α,p2,\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}\bigl(2\sigma\epsilon_{n,\alpha,p}/3,\,\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,p},\,\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\bigr)\leq\log|\mathcal{N}_{1}|+\log|\mathcal{N}_{2}|\leq C^{\prime}n\epsilon_{n,\alpha,p}^{2},
(67)
which proves the claim.
∎
Proof of Proposition 6.
The proof of Proposition 6 follows the same arguments as the proof of Proposition 4, but instead we apply Lemmas 20, 21 and 22 to verify the assumptions of Corollary 17.
∎
F.3 Proofs of Theorems 5 and 7
Proof of Theorem 5.
For any test distribution μ\mu on 𝒫β\mathcal{P}_{\beta} such that χ2​(μ,πβ)≤κ\chi^{2}(\mu,\pi_{\beta})\leq\kappa, we have
χ2​(μ,π)=𝔼π​{(d​μd​π)2}−1≤maxα∈𝒜⁡1λα⋅𝔼πβ​{(d​μd​πβ)2}−1≤(κ+1)​maxα∈𝒜​1λα−1≕κ′.\displaystyle\chi^{2}(\mu,\pi)=\mathbb{E}_{\pi}\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu}{\mathrm{d}\pi}\biggr)^{2}\biggr\}-1\leq\max_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{1}{\lambda_{\alpha}}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{\beta}}\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu}{\mathrm{d}\pi_{\beta}}\biggr)^{2}\biggr\}-1\leq(\kappa+1)\max_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{1}{\lambda_{\alpha}}-1\eqqcolon\kappa^{\prime}.
Now, by Proposition 1, then by taking dmodel∘≥d+2d_{\mathrm{model}}^{\circ}\geq d+2, dffn∘∈ℕd_{\mathrm{ffn}}^{\circ}\in\mathbb{N} and T∈ℕT\in\mathbb{N} large enough that Proposition 3 holds with ϵ=1/{16​R2​n2​(κ′+1)}\epsilon=1/\{16R^{2}n^{2}(\kappa^{\prime}+1)\} and applying Proposition 4, we have
ℛμICL​(f~T)\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mu}^{\mathrm{ICL}}(\widetilde{f}_{T})
≤4​R​{χ2​(μ,π)+1}​ℰ​(f~T)+2​𝔼μ​𝔼P​[{gπ​(X,𝒟n)−g∘​(X)}2]\displaystyle\leq 4R\sqrt{\bigl\{\chi^{2}(\mu,\pi)+1\bigr\}\mathcal{E}(\widetilde{f}_{T})}+2\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl[\bigl\{g_{\pi}(X,\mathcal{D}_{n})-g^{\circ}(X)\bigr\}^{2}\bigr]
≤1n+C′n−2β/(2β+d)≤Cn−2β/(2β+d),\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}+C^{\prime}n^{-2\beta/(2\beta+d)}\leq Cn^{-2\beta/(2\beta+d)},
where C′,C>0C^{\prime},C>0 depend on neither nn nor κ\kappa.
∎
Proof of Theorem 7.
As in the proof of Theorem 5, for any test distribution μ\mu on 𝒫β,r\mathcal{P}_{\beta,r} such that χ2​(μ,πβ,r)≤κ\chi^{2}(\mu,\pi_{\beta,r})\leq\kappa, we have χ2​(μ,π)≤(κ+1)​max(α,p)∈𝒜′​λα,p−1−1≕κ′\chi^{2}(\mu,\pi)\leq(\kappa+1)\max_{(\alpha,p)\in\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}\lambda_{\alpha,p}^{-1}-1\eqqcolon\kappa^{\prime}.
The result therefore follows by the same argument as in the proof of Theorem 5, except that we apply Proposition 6 in place of Proposition 4 to obtain the improved bound ℛμICL(f~T)≤Cn−2β/(2β+r)\mathcal{R}_{\mu}^{\mathrm{ICL}}(\widetilde{f}_{T})\leq Cn^{-2\beta/(2\beta+r)} for sufficiently large TT, where C>0C>0 depends on neither nn nor κ\kappa.
∎
F.4 Proof of Theorem 8
Proof of Theorem 8.
We use the Bayes risk lower bound techniques developed by Chen, Guntuboyina and Zhang (2016). Let (𝒫∗,𝒢∗)(\mathcal{P}_{*},\mathcal{G}_{*}) be either (𝒫β,𝒢β)(\mathcal{P}_{\beta},\mathcal{G}_{\beta}) defined as in Section 4.1 or (𝒫β,r,𝒢β,r)(\mathcal{P}_{\beta,r},\mathcal{G}_{\beta,r}) defined as in Section 4.2, and let μ~\widetilde{\mu} denote the distribution on 𝒢∗\mathcal{G}_{*} induced by μ\mu on 𝒫∗\mathcal{P}_{*}, so that μ~\widetilde{\mu} is the distribution of the random function x↦𝔼P​(Y|X=x)x\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{P}(Y\,|\,X=x), where the randomness comes from P∼μP\sim\mu. By Chen, Guntuboyina and Zhang (2016, Corollary 12(i) and Eq. (48)), we have
infg^n∈𝒢^nℛμICL​(g^n)\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{g}_{n}\in\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}}\mathcal{R}_{\mu}^{\mathrm{ICL}}(\widehat{g}_{n})
=infg^n∈𝒢^n𝔼P∼μ​𝔼𝒟n|P∼P⊗n​{‖g^n​(⋅,𝒟n)−gP‖L2​(PX)2}\displaystyle=\inf_{\widehat{g}_{n}\in\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}}\mathbb{E}_{P\sim\mu}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}|P\sim P^{\otimes n}}\bigl\{\|\widehat{g}_{n}(\cdot,\mathcal{D}_{n})-g_{P}\|_{L^{2}(P_{X})}^{2}\bigr\}
=infg^n∈𝒢^n𝔼g~∼μ~​𝔼𝒟n|g~∼Pg~⊗n​{‖g^n​(⋅,𝒟n)−g~‖L2​(PX)2}\displaystyle=\inf_{\widehat{g}_{n}\in\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\widetilde{g}\sim\widetilde{\mu}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{n}|\widetilde{g}\sim P_{\widetilde{g}}^{\otimes n}}\bigl\{\|\widehat{g}_{n}(\cdot,\mathcal{D}_{n})-\widetilde{g}\|_{L^{2}(P_{X})}^{2}\bigr\}
≥12​sup{t>0:supg∘∈𝒢∗μ~​({g∈𝒢∗:‖g−g∘‖L2​(PX)2<t})<14​e−2​IKLup},\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\sup\biggl\{t>0:\sup_{g^{\circ}\in\mathcal{G}_{*}}\widetilde{\mu}\Bigl(\bigl\{g\in\mathcal{G}_{*}:\|g-g^{\circ}\|_{L^{2}(P_{X})}^{2}<t\bigr\}\Bigr)<\frac{1}{4}e^{-2I^{\mathrm{up}}_{\mathrm{KL}}}\biggr\},
(68)
where
IKLup≔infη>0{log⁡𝒩⁡(η2/n,𝒫∗,KL)+η2}\displaystyle I^{\mathrm{up}}_{\mathrm{KL}}\coloneqq\inf_{\eta>0}\bigl\{\log\mathcal{N}(\eta^{2}/n,\mathcal{P}_{*},\mathrm{KL})+\eta^{2}\bigr\}
and we recall that 𝒩⁡(η2/n,𝒫∗,KL)\mathcal{N}(\eta^{2}/n,\mathcal{P}_{*},\mathrm{KL}) is the (η2/n)(\eta^{2}/n)-covering number of 𝒫∗\mathcal{P}_{*} with respect to the Kullback–Leibler divergence. By Lemma 24(a), we deduce that
IKLup≤infη>0{log𝒩(ση2/n,𝒢∗,∥⋅∥∞)+η2}.\displaystyle I^{\mathrm{up}}_{\mathrm{KL}}\leq\inf_{\eta>0}\bigl\{\log\mathcal{N}\bigl(\sigma\eta\sqrt{2/n},\mathcal{G}_{*},\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\bigr)+\eta^{2}\bigr\}.
From now on, C1,C2,…C_{1},C_{2},\ldots will denote positive quantities that do not depend on nn.
(a) By choosing η≔nd/(2​β+d)\eta\coloneqq n^{d/(2\beta+d)} and Giné and Nickl (2021, Eq. (4.184)), we deduce that
IKLup≤C1​nd/(2​β+d).\displaystyle I^{\mathrm{up}}_{\mathrm{KL}}\leq C_{1}n^{d/(2\beta+d)}.
By the measurable bijection between 𝒢β\mathcal{G}_{\beta} and 𝒫β\mathcal{P}_{\beta} and our assumption on μ\mu, we have that supg∈𝒢βd​μ~d​π~β​(g)<∞\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\beta}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\mu}}{\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\pi}_{\beta}}(g)<\infty.
Thus, by Lemma 19, there exists C2>0C_{2}>0 small enough that for all g∘∈𝒢βg^{\circ}\in\mathcal{G}_{\beta},
μ~({g∈𝒢β\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}\Bigl(\bigl\{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\beta}
