≤{supg∈𝒢βd​μ~d​π~β(g)}π~β({g∈𝒢β:∥g−g∘∥L2​(PX)2<C2n−2β/(2β+d)})<14e−2​IKLup.\displaystyle\leq\biggl\{\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\beta}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\mu}}{\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\pi}_{\beta}}(g)\biggr\}\widetilde{\pi}_{\beta}\Bigl(\bigl\{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\beta}:\|g-g^{\circ}\|_{L^{2}(P_{X})}^{2}<C_{2}n^{-2\beta/(2\beta+d)}\bigr\}\Bigr)<\frac{1}{4}e^{-2I^{\mathrm{up}}_{\mathrm{KL}}}.
The claim then follows from (68).
(b) By choosing η≔nd/(2​β+r)\eta\coloneqq n^{d/(2\beta+r)} and applying (67) with (β,r)(\beta,r) replacing (α,p)(\alpha,p), we deduce that
IKLup≤C3​nr/(2​β+r).\displaystyle I^{\mathrm{up}}_{\mathrm{KL}}\leq C_{3}n^{r/(2\beta+r)}.

μ~({g\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}\Bigl(\bigl\{g

≤{supg∈𝒢β,rd​μ~d​π~β,r(g)}π~β,r({g∈𝒢β,r:∥g−g∘∥L2​(PX)2<C4n−2β/(2β+r)})<14e−2​IKLup.\displaystyle\leq\biggl\{\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\beta,r}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\mu}}{\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\pi}_{\beta,r}}(g)\biggr\}\widetilde{\pi}_{\beta,r}\Bigl(\bigl\{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\beta,r}:\|g-g^{\circ}\|_{L^{2}(P_{X})}^{2}<C_{4}n^{-2\beta/(2\beta+r)}\bigr\}\Bigr)<\frac{1}{4}e^{-2I^{\mathrm{up}}_{\mathrm{KL}}}.
The claim then follows again from (68).
∎
Appendix G Auxiliary lemmas
The lemma below is a modification of Ghosal, Lember and van der Vaart (2008, Lemma 6.3), where we assume that the log-likelihood ratio is sub-Gamma in the left tail with uniform sub-Gamma parameters in order to obtain an exponential concentration.
Lemma 23.
Let 𝒫\mathcal{P} be a measurable space of distributions on a measurable space 𝒵\mathcal{Z}, let P0P_{0} be a distribution on 𝒵\mathcal{Z} and let Z1,…,Zn​∼iid​P0Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\overset{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}P_{0}. For ϵ>0\epsilon>0, define
B(P0,ϵ)≔{P∈𝒫:KL(P0,P)≤ϵ2,V2(P0,P)≤ϵ2}.\displaystyle B(P_{0},\epsilon)\coloneqq\Bigl\{P\in\mathcal{P}:\mathrm{KL}(P_{0},P)\leq\epsilon^{2},\,\mathrm{V}_{2}(P_{0},P)\leq\epsilon^{2}\Bigr\}.
For i∈[n]i\in[n] and P∈𝒫P\in\mathcal{P} with P≪P0P\ll P_{0}, let
W⁡(P,Zi)≔log⁡d​Pd​P0​(Zi)−𝔼​log⁡d​Pd​P0​(Zi).W(P,Z_{i})\coloneqq\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P}{\mathrm{d}P_{0}}(Z_{i})-\mathbb{E}\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P}{\mathrm{d}P_{0}}(Z_{i}).
Assume that there exist a,c>0a,c>0 such that for all P∈B⁡(P0,ϵ)P\in B(P_{0},\epsilon), we have that W⁡(P,Z1)W(P,Z_{1}) is sub-Gamma in the left tail with variance parameter a​ϵ2a\epsilon^{2} and scale parameter cc.
Then, for any probability measure Π\Pi on 𝒫\mathcal{P} and any ϵ,D>0\epsilon,D>0, we have with P0⊗nP_{0}^{\otimes n}-probability at most exp⁡(−D2​n​ϵ22​(a+c​D))\exp\bigl(-\frac{D^{2}n\epsilon^{2}}{2(a+cD)}\bigr) that
∫B⁡(P0,ϵ)∏i=1nd​Pd​P0​(Zi)​𝑑Π​(P)<Π⁡(B⁡(P0,ϵ))​e−(1+D)​n​ϵ2.\displaystyle\int_{B(P_{0},\epsilon)}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\mathrm{d}P}{\mathrm{d}P_{0}}(Z_{i})\,\mathrm{d}\Pi(P)<\Pi\bigl(B(P_{0},\epsilon)\bigr)e^{-(1+D)n\epsilon^{2}}.
(69)
Proof.
Writing B≔B⁡(P0,ϵ)B\coloneqq B(P_{0},\epsilon), we observe that the conclusion is clear if Π⁡(B)=0\Pi(B)=0 because the right-hand side of (69) is zero. Moreover, by replacing Π\Pi with its conditional distribution on BB if necessary, we may assume without loss of generality that Π⁡(B)=1\Pi(B)=1. Now, by Jensen’s inequality,
1n​log​∫B∏i=1nd​Pd​P0​(Zi)​𝑑Π​(P)\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\log\int_{B}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\mathrm{d}P}{\mathrm{d}P_{0}}(Z_{i})\,\mathrm{d}\Pi(P)
≥1n​∑i=1n∫Blog⁡d​Pd​P0​(Zi)​𝑑Π​(P)\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{B}\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P}{\mathrm{d}P_{0}}(Z_{i})\,\mathrm{d}\Pi(P)
=1n​∑i=1n∫BW⁡(P,Zi)​𝑑Π​(P)−∫BKL⁡(P0,P)​𝑑Π​(P)\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{B}W(P,Z_{i})\,\mathrm{d}\Pi(P)-\int_{B}\mathrm{KL}(P_{0},P)\,\mathrm{d}\Pi(P)
≥1n​∑i=1n∫BW⁡(P,Zi)​𝑑Π​(P)−ϵ2,\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{B}W(P,Z_{i})\,\mathrm{d}\Pi(P)-\epsilon^{2},
(70)
where the final inequality follows from the definition of BB. Moreover, for λ∈(−1/c,0]\lambda\in(-1/c,0], by Jensen’s inequality and Fubini’s theorem,
𝔼Z1​exp⁡{λ​∫BW⁡(P,Z1)​𝑑Π​(P)}\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Z_{1}}\exp\biggl\{\lambda\int_{B}W(P,Z_{1})\,\mathrm{d}\Pi(P)\biggr\}
≤𝔼Z1​[∫Bexp⁡{λ​W​(P,Z1)}​𝑑Π​(P)]\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{Z_{1}}\biggl[\int_{B}\exp\bigl\{\lambda W(P,Z_{1})\bigr\}\,\mathrm{d}\Pi(P)\biggr]
=∫B𝔼Z1​exp⁡{λ​W​(P,Z1)}​𝑑Π​(P)≤exp⁡(a​ϵ2​λ22​(1−c​λ)),\displaystyle=\int_{B}\mathbb{E}_{Z_{1}}\exp\bigl\{\lambda W(P,Z_{1})\bigr\}\,\mathrm{d}\Pi(P)\leq\exp\biggl(\frac{a\epsilon^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2(1-c\lambda)}\biggr),
where the final inequality follows from the sub-Gamma assumption on W⁡(P,Z1)W(P,Z_{1}). This shows that ∫BW⁡(P,Z1)​𝑑Π​(P)\int_{B}W(P,Z_{1})\,\mathrm{d}\Pi(P) is sub-Gamma in the left tail with variance parameter a​ϵ2a\epsilon^{2} and scale parameter cc, so n−1​∑i=1n∫BW⁡(P,Zi)​𝑑Π​(P)n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{B}W(P,Z_{i})\,\mathrm{d}\Pi(P) is also sub-Gamma in the left tail with variance parameter a​ϵ2/na\epsilon^{2}/n and scale parameter c/nc/n (e.g. Samworth and Shah, 2025+, Exercise 10.6.12). Hence, by (70),
ℙ(1nlog∫B∏i=1n\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\log\int_{B}\prod_{i=1}^{n}
OPENd​Pd​P0​(Zi)​d​Π​(P)<−(1+D)​ϵ2)\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}P}{\mathrm{d}P_{0}}(Z_{i})\,\mathrm{d}\Pi(P)<-(1+D)\epsilon^{2}\biggr)
≤ℙ⁡(1n​∑i=1n∫BW⁡(P,Zi)​𝑑Π​(P)<−D​ϵ2)≤exp⁡(−D2​n​ϵ22​(a+c​D)),\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{B}W(P,Z_{i})\,\mathrm{d}\Pi(P)<-D\epsilon^{2}\biggr)\leq\exp\biggl(-\frac{D^{2}n\epsilon^{2}}{2(a+cD)}\biggr),
where the final inequality follows from Samworth and Shah (2025+, Proposition 10.6.8(a)).
∎
We next derive some properties about the distributions of nonparametric regression models with Gaussian noise.
Lemma 24.
Let PXP_{X} be a Borel distribution on 𝒳⊆ℝd\mathcal{X}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}, let X∼PXX\sim P_{X} and let ξ∼N⁡(0,σ2)\xi\sim N(0,\sigma^{2}) be independent of XX. Further, let R>0R>0, g1,g2:𝒳→[−R,R]g_{1},g_{2}:\mathcal{X}\to[-R,R] be Borel measurable, let Y1=g1​(X)+ξY_{1}=g_{1}(X)+\xi and Y2=g2​(X)+ξY_{2}=g_{2}(X)+\xi. Finally, let P1P_{1} and P2P_{2} denote the distributions of (X,Y1)(X,Y_{1}) and (X,Y2)(X,Y_{2}) respectively. Then
(a)
KL⁡(P1,P2)=12​σ2​𝔼​{(g1​(X)−g2​(X))2}\mathrm{KL}(P_{1},P_{2})=\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\bigl(g_{1}(X)-g_{2}(X)\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}.
(b)
V2​(P1,P2)=12​σ2​Var​{(g1​(X)−g2​(X))2}+2​𝔼​{(g1​(X)−g2​(X))2}\mathrm{V}_{2}(P_{1},P_{2})=\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\mathrm{Var}\bigl\{\bigl(g_{1}(X)-g_{2}(X)\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}+2\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\bigl(g_{1}(X)-g_{2}(X)\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}.
(c)
If KL⁡(P1,P2)≤ϵ2\mathrm{KL}(P_{1},P_{2})\leq\epsilon^{2} and V2​(P1,P2)≤ϵ2\mathrm{V}_{2}(P_{1},P_{2})\leq\epsilon^{2}, then W≔log⁡d​P2d​P1​(X,Y1)−𝔼​log⁡d​P2d​P1​(X,Y1)W\coloneqq\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{2}}{\mathrm{d}P_{1}}(X,Y_{1})-\mathbb{E}\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{2}}{\mathrm{d}P_{1}}(X,Y_{1}) is sub-Gamma in both tails with variance parameter (8∨2σ2)​ϵ2\bigl(8\vee\frac{2}{\sigma^{2}}\bigr)\epsilon^{2} and scale parameter c≔2​Rσ∨4​R23​σ2c\coloneqq\frac{2R}{\sigma}\vee\frac{4R^{2}}{3\sigma^{2}}.
(d)
1−e−R2/(2σ2)2​R2⋅𝔼⁡{(g1​(X)−g2​(X))2}≤dH2​(P1,P2)≤14​σ2⋅𝔼⁡{(g1​(X)−g2​(X))2}\frac{1-e^{-R^{2}/(2\sigma^{2})}}{2R^{2}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\bigl(g_{1}(X)-g_{2}(X)\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}\leq d_{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(P_{1},P_{2})\leq\frac{1}{4\sigma^{2}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\bigl(g_{1}(X)-g_{2}(X)\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}.
Proof.
(a) Let PY1|XP_{Y_{1}|X} and PY2|XP_{Y_{2}|X} denote the conditional distributions of Y1Y_{1} given XX and Y2Y_{2} given XX respectively. Then, using the fact that Y1=g1​(X)+ξY_{1}=g_{1}(X)+\xi,
log⁡d​P2d​P1​(X,Y1)=log⁡d​PY2|Xd​PY1|X​(X,Y1)\displaystyle\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{2}}{\mathrm{d}P_{1}}(X,Y_{1})=\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{Y_{2}|X}}{\mathrm{d}P_{Y_{1}|X}}(X,Y_{1})
=−(Y1−g2​(X))22​σ2+(Y1−g1​(X))22​σ2\displaystyle=-\frac{\bigl(Y_{1}-g_{2}(X)\bigr)^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}+\frac{\bigl(Y_{1}-g_{1}(X)\bigr)^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}
=−(g1​(X)−g2​(X))2−2​(g1​(X)−g2​(X))​ξ2​σ2.\displaystyle=\frac{-\bigl(g_{1}(X)-g_{2}(X)\bigr)^{2}-2\bigl(g_{1}(X)-g_{2}(X)\bigr)\xi}{2\sigma^{2}}.
(71)
Hence,
KL⁡(P1,P2)=−𝔼​log⁡d​P2d​P1​(X,Y1)=12​σ2​𝔼​{(g1​(X)−g2​(X))2}.\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(P_{1},P_{2})=-\mathbb{E}\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{2}}{\mathrm{d}P_{1}}(X,Y_{1})=\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\bigl(g_{1}(X)-g_{2}(X)\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}.
(72)
(b) By (71) and (72), we have
V2​(P1,P2)\displaystyle\mathrm{V}_{2}(P_{1},P_{2})
=12​σ2​𝔼​[{(g1​(X)−g2​(X))2+2​(g1​(X)−g2​(X))​ξ−𝔼⁡{(g1​(X)−g2​(X))2}}2]\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl\{\bigl(g_{1}(X)-g_{2}(X)\bigr)^{2}+2\bigl(g_{1}(X)-g_{2}(X)\bigr)\xi-\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\bigl(g_{1}(X)-g_{2}(X)\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}\Bigr\}^{2}\Bigr]
=12​σ2​Var​{(g1​(X)−g2​(X))2}+2​𝔼​{(g1​(X)−g2​(X))2},\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\mathrm{Var}\bigl\{\bigl(g_{1}(X)-g_{2}(X)\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}+2\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\bigl(g_{1}(X)-g_{2}(X)\bigr)^{2}\bigr\},
where in the final equality, we used the fact that ξ⟂⟂X\xi\perp\!\!\!\perp X, 𝔼⁡(ξ)=0\mathbb{E}(\xi)=0 and 𝔼⁡(ξ2)=σ2\mathbb{E}(\xi^{2})=\sigma^{2}.
(c) From (71) and (72), we deduce that
W\displaystyle W
=log⁡d​P2d​P1​(X,Y1)−𝔼​log⁡d​P2d​P1​(X,Y1)\displaystyle=\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{2}}{\mathrm{d}P_{1}}(X,Y_{1})-\mathbb{E}\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{2}}{\mathrm{d}P_{1}}(X,Y_{1})
=(g2​(X)−g1​(X))​ξσ2−(g1​(X)−g2​(X))2−𝔼⁡{(g1​(X)−g2​(X))2}2​σ2≕W1+W2.\displaystyle=\frac{\bigl(g_{2}(X)-g_{1}(X)\bigr)\xi}{\sigma^{2}}-\frac{\bigl(g_{1}(X)-g_{2}(X)\bigr)^{2}-\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\bigl(g_{1}(X)-g_{2}(X)\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}}{2\sigma^{2}}\eqqcolon W_{1}+W_{2}.
For λ∈[0,σ/R)\lambda\in[0,\sigma/R), using the fact that log⁡(1+x)≤x\log(1+x)\leq x for x≥0x\geq 0 and Fubini’s theorem,
log⁡𝔼​eλ​W1\displaystyle\log\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda W_{1}}
=log⁡𝔼​exp⁡(λ⁡(g2​(X)−g1​(X))​ξσ2)=log⁡𝔼​exp⁡(λ2​(g2​(X)−g1​(X))22​σ2)\displaystyle=\log\mathbb{E}\exp\biggl(\frac{\lambda\bigl(g_{2}(X)-g_{1}(X)\bigr)\xi}{\sigma^{2}}\biggr)=\log\mathbb{E}\exp\biggl(\frac{\lambda^{2}\bigl(g_{2}(X)-g_{1}(X)\bigr)^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\biggr)
≤∑r=1∞λ2​r​𝔼​{(g2​(X)−g1​(X))2​r}(2​σ2)r⋅r!≤𝔼⁡{(g2​(X)−g1​(X))2}​∑r=1∞λ2​r​(2​R)2​r−2(2​σ2)r⋅r!\displaystyle\leq\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\lambda^{2r}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\bigl(g_{2}(X)-g_{1}(X)\bigr)^{2r}\bigr\}}{(2\sigma^{2})^{r}\cdot r!}\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\bigl(g_{2}(X)-g_{1}(X)\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\lambda^{2r}(2R)^{2r-2}}{(2\sigma^{2})^{r}\cdot r!}
≤λ2​ϵ2​∑r=1∞2r−1r!​(λ​Rσ)2​r−2≤λ2​ϵ2​∑r=1∞(λ​Rσ)r−1=2​λ2​ϵ22​(1−λ​Rσ).\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{2}\epsilon^{2}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{2^{r-1}}{r!}\biggl(\frac{\lambda R}{\sigma}\biggr)^{2r-2}\leq\lambda^{2}\epsilon^{2}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\biggl(\frac{\lambda R}{\sigma}\biggr)^{r-1}=\frac{2\lambda^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{2\bigl(1-\frac{\lambda R}{\sigma}\bigr)}.
The same holds true if W1W_{1} is replaced by −W1-W_{1}.
Thus W1W_{1} is sub-Gamma in both tails with variance parameter 2​ϵ22\epsilon^{2} and scale parameter R/σR/\sigma. By part (b) and the assumption that V2​(P1,P2)≤ϵ2\mathrm{V}_{2}(P_{1},P_{2})\leq\epsilon^{2}, we have 𝔼⁡(W22)≤ϵ2/(2​σ2)\mathbb{E}(W_{2}^{2})\leq\epsilon^{2}/(2\sigma^{2}). Moreover, since W2W_{2} is bounded by 2​R2/σ22R^{2}/\sigma^{2}, we have for r≥3r\geq 3 that
𝔼⁡(|W2|r)≤𝔼⁡(W22)⋅(2​R2σ2)r−2≤r!​ϵ22⋅2​σ2​(2​R23​σ2)r−2.\mathbb{E}(|W_{2}|^{r})\leq\mathbb{E}(W_{2}^{2})\cdot\Bigl(\frac{2R^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Bigr)^{r-2}\leq\frac{r!\epsilon^{2}}{2\cdot 2\sigma^{2}}\Bigl(\frac{2R^{2}}{3\sigma^{2}}\Bigr)^{r-2}.
The same bound holds for −W2-W_{2}, so by Samworth and Shah (2025+, Proposition 10.6.8(b)), W2W_{2} is sub-Gamma in both tails with variance parameter ϵ22​σ2\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}} and scale parameter 2​R23​σ2\frac{2R^{2}}{3\sigma^{2}}. Finally, by Samworth and Shah (2025+, Exercise 10.6.12), we have that W=W1+W2W=W_{1}+W_{2} is sub-Gamma in both tails with variance parameter (8∨2σ2)​ϵ2\bigl(8\vee\frac{2}{\sigma^{2}}\bigr)\epsilon^{2} and scale parameter c=2​Rσ∨4​R23​σ2c=\frac{2R}{\sigma}\vee\frac{4R^{2}}{3\sigma^{2}}.
(d) We have
dH2​(P1,P2)=𝔼​dH2​(PY1|X,PY2|X)\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(P_{1},P_{2})=\mathbb{E}\,d_{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(P_{Y_{1}|X},P_{Y_{2}|X})
=2​𝔼​{1−exp⁡(−(g1​(X)−g2​(X))28​σ2)}\displaystyle=2\,\mathbb{E}\biggl\{1-\exp\biggl(-\frac{\bigl(g_{1}(X)-g_{2}(X)\bigr)^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}}\biggr)\biggr\}
≥1−e−R2/(2σ2)2​R2⋅𝔼⁡{(g1​(X)−g2​(X))2},\displaystyle\geq\frac{1-e^{-R^{2}/(2\sigma^{2})}}{2R^{2}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\bigl(g_{1}(X)-g_{2}(X)\bigr)^{2}\bigr\},
where the inequality follows since x↦(1−e−x)/xx\mapsto(1-e^{-x})/x is decreasing on (0,∞)(0,\infty) and moreover (g1​(X)−g2​(X))28​σ2≤R22​σ2\frac{(g_{1}(X)-g_{2}(X))^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}}\leq\frac{R^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}. On the other hand, using the fact that 1−e−x≤x1-e^{-x}\leq x for x≥0x\geq 0, we also have
2​𝔼​{1−exp⁡(−(g1​(X)−g2​(X))28​σ2)}≤14​σ2⋅𝔼⁡{(g1​(X)−g2​(X))2},\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\biggl\{1-\exp\biggl(-\frac{\bigl(g_{1}(X)-g_{2}(X)\bigr)^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}}\biggr)\biggr\}\leq\frac{1}{4\sigma^{2}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\bigl(g_{1}(X)-g_{2}(X)\bigr)^{2}\bigr\},
as required.
∎
Lemma 25.
Suppose f∈L2​([0,1]d)f\in L^{2}([0,1]^{d}) has wavelet decomposition
f=∑k∈Kak​Φk+∑ℓ=ℓ0∞∑γ∈Γℓbℓ,γ​Ψℓ,γ.\displaystyle f=\sum_{k\in K}a_{k}\Phi_{k}+\sum_{\ell=\ell_{0}}^{\infty}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{\ell}}b_{\ell,\gamma}\Psi_{\ell,\gamma}.
Let 0≤c1<c2≤10\leq c_{1}<c_{2}\leq 1 and let Λ≔{(ℓ,γ):supp⁡(Ψℓ,γ)⊆[c1,c2]d}\Lambda\coloneqq\bigl\{(\ell,\gamma):\mathrm{supp}(\Psi_{\ell,\gamma})\subseteq[c_{1},c_{2}]^{d}\bigr\}. Then
‖f‖L2​([c1,c2]d)2≥∑(ℓ,γ)∈Λbℓ,γ2.\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{2}([c_{1},c_{2}]^{d})}^{2}\geq\sum_{(\ell,\gamma)\in\Lambda}b^{2}_{\ell,\gamma}.
Proof.
Note that supp⁡(Ψℓ,γ)⊆[c1,c2]d\mathrm{supp}(\Psi_{\ell,\gamma})\subseteq[c_{1},c_{2}]^{d} for all (ℓ,γ)∈Λ(\ell,\gamma)\in\Lambda, so by the orthonormality of the wavelet basis, we have that (Ψℓ,γ)(ℓ,γ)∈Λ(\Psi_{\ell,\gamma})_{(\ell,\gamma)\in\Lambda} is also a sequence of orthonormal functions in the Hilbert space L2​([c1,c2]d)L^{2}([c_{1},c_{2}]^{d}) equipped with the inner product
⟨f1,f2⟩[c1,c2]d≔∫[c1,c2]df1​(x)​f2​(x)​𝑑x.\displaystyle\langle f_{1},f_{2}\rangle_{[c_{1},c_{2}]^{d}}\coloneqq\int_{[c_{1},c_{2}]^{d}}f_{1}(x)f_{2}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x.
Moreover, for (ℓ,γ)∈Λ(\ell,\gamma)\in\Lambda, we have
⟨f,Ψℓ,γ⟩[c1,c2]d=∫[c1,c2]df⁡(x)​Ψℓ,γ​(x)​𝑑x=∫[0,1]df⁡(x)​Ψℓ,γ​(x)​𝑑x=bℓ,γ.\displaystyle\langle f,\Psi_{\ell,\gamma}\rangle_{[c_{1},c_{2}]^{d}}=\int_{[c_{1},c_{2}]^{d}}f(x)\Psi_{\ell,\gamma}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{[0,1]^{d}}f(x)\Psi_{\ell,\gamma}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=b_{\ell,\gamma}.
Thus, an application of Bessel’s inequality (e.g. Rudin, 1987, Theorem 4.17) yields the desired result.
∎
Lemma 26.
Let U(r)U^{(r)} be uniformly distributed on the Stiefel manifold Vr​(ℝd)V_{r}(\mathbb{R}^{d}) and let U∈Vr​(ℝd)U\in V_{r}(\mathbb{R}^{d}) be deterministic. Then for any ϵ>0\epsilon>0, there exists c>0c>0, depending only on dd and rr, such that
ℙ⁡(‖U(r)−U‖op≤ϵ)≥c​(ϵ∧12)d​r.\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|U^{(r)}-U\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\epsilon\bigr)\geq c\biggl(\epsilon\wedge\frac{1}{2}\biggr)^{dr}.
Proof.
We may assume that ϵ≤1/2\epsilon\leq 1/2.
By Chikuse (2003, Theorem 2.2.1(iii)), we may assume without loss of generality that U(r)=Z(Z⊤Z)−1/2≕F(Z)U^{(r)}=Z(Z^{\top}Z)^{-1/2}\eqqcolon F(Z), where ZZ is a d×rd\times r random matrix with independent N⁡(0,1)N(0,1) entries. Let Δ∈ℝd×r\Delta\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r} be such that δ≔‖Δ‖op≤1/6\delta\coloneqq\|\Delta\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1/6, and define
A≔U⊤​Δ+Δ⊤​U+Δ⊤​Δ∈ℝr×r.\displaystyle A\coloneqq U^{\top}\Delta+\Delta^{\top}U+\Delta^{\top}\Delta\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}.
Then ‖A‖op≤3​δ≤1/2\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 3\delta\leq 1/2, so (U+Δ)⊤​(U+Δ)=Ir+A(U+\Delta)^{\top}(U+\Delta)=I_{r}+A is positive definite. Thus
‖F⁡(U+Δ)−U‖op\displaystyle\|F(U+\Delta)-U\|_{\mathrm{op}}
=∥(U+Δ)(Ir+A)−1/2−U∥op\displaystyle=\|(U+\Delta)(I_{r}+A)^{-1/2}-U\|_{\mathrm{op}}
≤∥U∥op∥(Ir+A)−1/2−Ir∥op+∥Δ∥op∥(Ir+A)−1/2∥op.\displaystyle\leq\|U\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|(I_{r}+A)^{-1/2}-I_{r}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|\Delta\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|(I_{r}+A)^{-1/2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}.
Moreover, we can write Ir+A=V​D​V⊤I_{r}+A=VDV^{\top} where V∈ℝr×rV\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r} is orthogonal and D∈ℝr×rD\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r} is diagonal with diagonal elements bounded between 1−3​δ1-3\delta and 1+3​δ1+3\delta. Thus, ∥(Ir+A)−1/2−Ir∥op≤3δ\|(I_{r}+A)^{-1/2}-I_{r}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 3\delta and ∥(Ir+A)−1/2∥op≤2\|(I_{r}+A)^{-1/2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2, so
‖F⁡(U+Δ)−U‖op≤3​δ+2​δ=5​δ.\displaystyle\|F(U+\Delta)-U\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 3\delta+2\delta=5\delta.
Hence, if ‖Z−U‖op≤ϵ/5<1/6\|Z-U\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\epsilon/5<1/6, then ‖F⁡(Z)−U‖op≤ϵ\|F(Z)-U\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\epsilon, so
ℙ(∥U(r)\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|U^{(r)}
−U∥op≤ϵ)=ℙ(∥F(Z)−U∥op≤ϵ)≥ℙ(∥Z−U∥op≤ϵ5)\displaystyle-U\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\epsilon\bigr)=\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|F(Z)-U\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\epsilon\bigr)\geq\mathbb{P}\biggl(\|Z-U\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{5}\biggr)
≥ℙ⁡(‖Z−U‖∞≤ϵ5​d​r)≥{ℙ⁡(|Z11−1|≤ϵ5​d​r)}d​r≥(2​ϵ25​d​r)d​r,\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\biggl(\|Z-U\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{5\sqrt{dr}}\biggr)\geq\biggl\{\mathbb{P}\biggl(|Z_{11}-1|\leq\frac{\epsilon}{5\sqrt{dr}}\biggr)\biggr\}^{dr}\geq\biggl(\frac{2\epsilon}{25\sqrt{dr}}\biggr)^{dr},
where the third inequality follows since ‖U‖∞≤1\|U\|_{\infty}\leq 1 and ZZ has independent N⁡(0,1)N(0,1) entries, and the final inequality follows since ϵ5​d​r≤110\frac{\epsilon}{5\sqrt{dr}}\leq\frac{1}{10} and the standard normal density is bounded below by 1/51/5 on the interval [0.9,1.1][0.9,1.1].
∎
Lemma 27.
Let PXP_{X} be a distribution on 𝔹d\mathbb{B}^{d} that satisfies the assumptions of Lemma 21. Let p∈[d]p\in[d], U∈Vp​(ℝd)U\in V_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d}), X∼PXX\sim P_{X} and let QQ denote the distribution of U⊤​X+𝟏p2\frac{U^{\top}X+\bm{1}_{p}}{2}. Then there exist c0′≡c0′​(c0,τ,d,p)c_{0}^{\prime}\equiv c_{0}^{\prime}(c_{0},\tau,d,p) and a hypercube A′⊆[0,1]pA^{\prime}\subseteq[0,1]^{p} of side length τ2​p\frac{\tau}{2\sqrt{p}} with the property that Q⁡(A0′)≥c0′​Volp​(A0′)Q(A_{0}^{\prime})\geq c_{0}^{\prime}\mathrm{Vol}_{p}(A_{0}^{\prime}) for all measurable A0′⊆A′A_{0}^{\prime}\subseteq A^{\prime}.
Proof.
If p=dp=d, then U∈ℝd×dU\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d} is orthogonal, so for all measurable A0⊆AA_{0}\subseteq A,
Q⁡(U⊤​A0+𝟏d2)=ℙ⁡(X∈A0)≥c0​Vold​(A0).Q\biggl(\frac{U^{\top}A_{0}+\bm{1}_{d}}{2}\biggr)=\mathbb{P}(X\in A_{0})\geq c_{0}\mathrm{Vol}_{d}(A_{0}).
Moreover, (U⊤​A+𝟏d)/2(U^{\top}A+\bm{1}_{d})/2 is a Euclidean ball of radius τ/2\tau/2, so it contains a hypercube of side length τ2​p\frac{\tau}{2\sqrt{p}}. Now suppose that p∈[d−1]p\in[d-1]. For v=(v1,…,vd)⊤∈ℝdv=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{d})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d} and 1≤j1≤j2≤d1\leq j_{1}\leq j_{2}\leq d, we write vj1:j2≔(vj1,…,vj2)⊤∈ℝj1−j2+1v_{j_{1}:j_{2}}\coloneqq(v_{j_{1}},\ldots,v_{j_{2}})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{j_{1}-j_{2}+1}.
Let o∈ℝdo\in\mathbb{R}^{d} be the centre of AA and let K⊆AK\subseteq A be the Euclidean ball of radius τ/2\tau/2 and centred at oo. We may write U=V​PU=VP where V∈ℝd×dV\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d} is orthogonal and P∈ℝd×pP\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times p} is the first pp columns of IdI_{d}. Let S⊆KS\subseteq K be Borel measurable, and define T≔{x∈V⊤A:x1:p∈P⊤V⊤S}T\coloneqq\{x\in V^{\top}A:x_{1:p}\in P^{\top}V^{\top}S\}. Then V​T⊆AVT\subseteq A; moreover, if y=(V⊤x)1:py=(V^{\top}x)_{1:p} for some x∈S⊆Kx\in S\subseteq K and z∈ℝd−pz\in\mathbb{R}^{d-p} satisfies ∥z−(V⊤o)(p+1):d∥2≤τ/2\|z-(V^{\top}o)_{(p+1):d}\|_{2}\leq\tau/2, then by the choice of KK,
∥(yz)−V⊤o∥22=∥y−(V⊤o)1:p∥22+∥z−(V⊤o)(p+1):d∥22≤(τ/2)2+(τ/2)2<τ2,\displaystyle\biggl\|\begin{pmatrix}y\\
z\end{pmatrix}-V^{\top}o\biggr\|_{2}^{2}=\|y-(V^{\top}o)_{1:p}\|_{2}^{2}+\|z-(V^{\top}o)_{(p+1):d}\|_{2}^{2}\leq(\tau/2)^{2}+(\tau/2)^{2}<\tau^{2},
so (y⊤,z⊤)⊤∈T(y^{\top},z^{\top})^{\top}\in T. Thus
T′≔{(y⊤,z⊤)⊤∈ℝp+(d−p):y∈P⊤V⊤S,∥z−(V⊤o)(p+1):d∥2≤τ/2}⊆T.\displaystyle T^{\prime}\coloneqq\bigl\{(y^{\top},z^{\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{p+(d-p)}:y\in P^{\top}V^{\top}S,\,\|z-(V^{\top}o)_{(p+1):d}\|_{2}\leq\tau/2\bigr\}\subseteq T.
(73)
Therefore, writing v⁡(τ,d,p)v(\tau,d,p) for the Lebesgue measure of a Euclidean ball in ℝd−p\mathbb{R}^{d-p} of radius τ/2\tau/2, we deduce that
Q⁡(U⊤​S+𝟏p2)\displaystyle Q\biggl(\frac{U^{\top}S+\bm{1}_{p}}{2}\biggr)
=ℙ⁡(P⊤​V⊤​X+𝟏p2∈P⊤​V⊤​S+𝟏p2)\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{P^{\top}V^{\top}X+\bm{1}_{p}}{2}\in\frac{P^{\top}V^{\top}S+\bm{1}_{p}}{2}\biggr)
≥ℙ⁡(V⊤​X∈T)=ℙ⁡(X∈V​T)≥c0​Vold​(V​T)=c0​Vold​(T)≥c0​Vold​(T′)\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(V^{\top}X\in T)=\mathbb{P}(X\in VT)\geq c_{0}\mathrm{Vol}_{d}(VT)=c_{0}\mathrm{Vol}_{d}(T)\geq c_{0}\mathrm{Vol}_{d}(T^{\prime})
=c0∫y∈P⊤​V⊤​S∫z∈ℝd−p:∥z−(V⊤o)(p+1):d∥2≤τ/2dzdy\displaystyle=c_{0}\int_{y\in P^{\top}V^{\top}S}\int_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d-p}:\|z-(V^{\top}o)_{(p+1):d}\|_{2}\leq\tau/2}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}y
=c0​v​(τ,d,p)​Volp​(P⊤​V⊤​S)≕c0′​Volp​(U⊤​S+𝟏p2).\displaystyle=c_{0}v(\tau,d,p)\mathrm{Vol}_{p}(P^{\top}V^{\top}S)\eqqcolon c_{0}^{\prime}\mathrm{Vol}_{p}\biggl(\frac{U^{\top}S+\bm{1}_{p}}{2}\biggr).
Since this holds for all measurable S⊆KS\subseteq K, we deduce that Q⁡(A0′)≥c0′​Volp​(A0′)Q(A_{0}^{\prime})\geq c_{0}^{\prime}\mathrm{Vol}_{p}(A_{0}^{\prime}) for all A0′⊆U⊤​K+𝟏p2A_{0}^{\prime}\subseteq\frac{U^{\top}K+\bm{1}_{p}}{2}. Now U⊤​K+𝟏p2\frac{U^{\top}K+\bm{1}_{p}}{2} is a ball of radius τ/4\tau/4 in ℝp\mathbb{R}^{p}, so it contains a hypercube A′A^{\prime} of side length τ2​p\frac{\tau}{2\sqrt{p}}.
∎

all.
